| **Notes** | **Session opened by discovering undocumented, uncommitted work-in-progress** from an unfinished prior session: `supabase/migrations/20260804110000_contact_messages.sql` (a real, well-formed `contact_messages` table + RLS migration, citing `database.md §5.16`, `api-design.md §5.12`, and "ADR-034" — **none of which existed**), plus two untracked debug scripts (`frontend/analyze-chunk.cjs`, `frontend/check-contrast.cjs`, likely Sprint 8 bundle-analysis/contrast-check leftovers). The developer confirmed a prior session had been asked to "find out what missing pages we didn't have and plan for their implementation" and instead jumped straight to an unapproved, unfinished, undocumented schema change — skipping `CLAUDE.md` §6/§8's Requirements → Architecture → Database ordering entirely. **Developer decision: discard the orphaned migration** (deleted this session) rather than adopt or repair it; the two leftover scripts were left in place, undecided (see Technical Debt). Proper survey then done directly: checked `routes/routes.tsx`, `paths.constants.ts`, `Footer.tsx`/`Header.tsx`/`MobileNavDrawer.tsx` for dead links (none — `Footer.tsx` is intentionally empty, flagged since Sprint 1), and cross-checked `requirements.md`/`user-stories.md`/`roadmap.md` for any existing scope (none). Confirmed gaps: About/How-It-Works, Contact (+ admin review), Terms of Service, Privacy Policy, and the catch-all `*` route's misleading `PlaceholderPage` copy ("Route skeleton — page not yet implemented") standing in for a genuine 404. **Two product decisions resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before drafting anything:** Contact delivery — **admin-review-only, no outbound email** (matches the deleted migration's own original call, avoids new email-provider infra); roadmap placement — **folded into Sprint 9 (Production Launch) as pre-launch prep scope**, not a dedicated new sprint (avoids renumbering the already-written Sprint 9 section). Documented, in the same change: `requirements.md` new §5.4 "Static & Legal Content" (`FR-CONTENT-001`–`005`, inserted after §5.3 Property Listings, before §6 Property Details); `user-stories.md` new Epic 11 "Static & Legal Content" (`CONTENT-001`–`005`, full User Story + Acceptance Criteria format matching every other epic), MVP Scope Summary table row, and Story-to-Epic Traceability Table rows; `roadmap.md` §13 (Sprint 9) gained a new Scope bullet and a new Release Checklist line gating `v1.0.0` on real (not placeholder) Terms/Privacy legal text. No `database.md`/`api-design.md`/`architecture.md` change yet — deliberately deferred to the implementation pass per `CLAUDE.md` §6 (Database/API docs get written when the feature is actually built, not spec'd speculatively ahead of it), and per the developer's explicit "plan first" scope for this session.

**Build phase (same session, developer said "build"):** Implemented `CONTENT-001`/`002`/`003`/`005` end to end, deliberately skipping `CONTENT-004` (Terms/Privacy) since it's still blocked on real legal text. New migration `supabase/migrations/20260805090000_contact_messages.sql`: `contact_messages` table + full RLS Policy Summary row (anyone INSERT, admin-only SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE — not moderator), plus a `count_recent_contact_messages_by_email()` `security definer` RPC added after a real test failure (below). New `entities/contact-message/` slice (repository has two real consumers — `features/contact`'s public submit, `features/admin-messages`'s review screen — per the ADR-026/028 "2+ consumers" test). New `features/contact/` (Zod schema, Service-layer rate limit, `useSubmitContactMessage`, `ContactForm` — pre-fills name/email for a signed-in user via a small feature-local `currentUserEmail` repository, since sibling features can't cross-import `features/profile-management`'s existing `getCurrentEmail()`). New `features/admin-messages/` (list/resolve/delete, mirroring `features/admin-activity-log`'s exact shape — no confirm dialog on delete, matching that precedent). Four new pages (`AboutPage` reuses `UserDashboardOverviewPage`'s welcome-block content verbatim for guests; `ContactPage`; `AdminMessagesPage`; `NotFoundPage` — implements `ui-guidelines.md` §19's already-documented "404" `EmptyState` row for the first time). `routes.tsx`/`paths.constants.ts` wired (all four lazy-loaded, matching every other route); `Footer.tsx` gets its first-ever real links (About/Contact only — Terms/Privacy deliberately not linked until they exist); `AdminDashboardLayout` nav gains "Messages."

**Two real, non-obvious RLS bugs found via failing integration tests, not assumed from the policy table, both from the same root cause (no role but admin can read `contact_messages` at all, not even a submitter's own row) — full detail and general rules now in `database.md` §9:** (1) `contactMessageRepository.submit()`'s first draft chained `.select().single()` after `.insert()` (this project's usual pattern) — failed with `42501`, since PostgREST's return-representation requires the caller to read back what they wrote, which a guest/customer can never do here. Fixed: `submit()` returns `void`. (2) The rate-limit check's first draft used a plain `.select('*', { count: 'exact', head: true })` — failed with a bare `401` for `anon`, since even a headless count-only request needs the table SELECT grant. Fixed with a new narrow `security definer` RPC, `count_recent_contact_messages_by_email()`, mirroring `current_role()`/`property_ids_with_all_amenities()`'s existing pattern. Both fixes verified via real integration tests against a freshly `db reset` local stack, not assumed from the code alone.

**Environment note:** local Supabase `db reset` hit the same `storage`/`vector`-container-unhealthy issue already logged in Sprint 8/session-management-hardening — resolved the same way, a full `supabase stop` + `start` + `db reset` cycle, not investigated further as its own fix.

Verified: 19 new tests, all passing (`contact-message.repository.test.ts` unit, `contact-message.rls.test.ts` — 4 real-Supabase RLS integration tests including the two bugs above, `contact.service.test.ts` — rate-limit + validation, `ContactForm.test.tsx` — real-Supabase integration covering guest submit/validation/signed-in prefill). Full suite: 364/365, the one failure (`PropertyAnalyticsChart`'s lazy-load test) confirmed passing in isolation — the same pre-existing full-suite-contention flakiness class documented since Sprint 6, unrelated to this work. `npm run lint` (0 errors, 38 pre-existing-style warnings, up from 27 only because of the new lazy route consts following the exact same existing pattern), `tsc -b` (clean), `npm run build` (clean — each new page confirmed in its own lazy chunk, e.g. `AboutPage-*.js` 2.36 kB, `ContactPage-*.js` 4.13 kB, `AdminMessagesPage-*.js` 3.71 kB, `NotFoundPage-*.js` 0.95 kB — no eager-bundle regression). `database.md` (§5.16 new table + two new §9 pitfall notes), `api-design.md` (§23 new Contact API section — inserted before the old §23, which is now §24 Claude Code Implementation Rules; two `coding-standards.md` cross-references to that section number updated in the same change), `ui-guidelines.md` (§24 new Static & Legal Content Pages section, §15.9 new Footer section), and `decisions.md` (new **ADR-034** — admin-review-only, no outbound email provider) all updated in the same change. **Manual real-browser verification could not be completed** — `claude-in-chrome` failed to connect at all (`tabs_context_mcp` returned "Browser extension is not connected") — a different, more severe failure than the viewport/screenshot-reporting bug logged since Sprint 4; stated explicitly rather than silently skipped or assumed passing. The dev server was left running (`localhost:5199`) for the developer to verify directly. Local Supabase stack left running (not reset/stopped) since the dev server is still up against it. |

## Sprint 2 — Authentication ✅

- [x] `AUTH-001` — Register with Email and Password — 2026-07-24. Foundation ticket for the sprint — see `tasks/Sprint-02/AUTH-001-register.md` for full detail. Added the Supabase CLI (repo-root dev dependency) and the project's first real migration (`supabase/migrations/20260724085140_auth_foundation.sql`): `user_role` enum, `profiles`/`roles` tables (+ 4-row `roles` seed), `handle_new_user` trigger, `prevent_self_role_change` trigger, `current_role()` RLS helper, and RLS policies for `profiles`/`roles` (`database.md` §9's first two Policy Summary rows) — deliberately scoped to just what Sprint 2 needs; `agencies`/`agents`/`current_agency_id()` are not created yet. Built the shared error-handling infrastructure every Repository now uses: `AppError`/`mapSupabaseError()`/`parseOrThrow()` (`shared/lib/errors`). Added shadcn `Input`/`Label`/`Alert`/`Sonner` primitives and a hand-built `FieldError`; gave `Button` an `isLoading` prop. Built `entities/user` (`Profile`/`Session` types, mapper, schema, `profile.repository.ts`, and the session-observing `AuthProvider`/`useAuth()` Context). Built `features/authentication`'s `auth.repository.ts` (full `AuthRepository` interface), `auth.service.ts`, `RegisterSchema`, `useRegister`, `RegisterForm`, wired into a real `RegisterPage`. Relocated `PATHS` from `routes/paths.constants.ts` to `shared/config/paths.constants.ts` since `pages/`/`features/` need it but cannot import from `routes/`. **Two real, non-obvious bugs found and fixed during verification** (not visible from reading `database.md` alone): (1) a fresh Supabase-CLI-created table has *no* table-level `GRANT` for `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` beyond `Dxtm` — RLS policies never even get evaluated without an explicit `GRANT` first; added the grants matching exactly what the Policy Summary already implies. (2) `service_role` requests have no `auth.uid()`, so `current_role()` reads `NULL` for them, which made `prevent_self_role_change` wrongly block `service_role` role-changes too (`NULL IS DISTINCT FROM 'admin'` is `true`); fixed with an explicit `auth.role() = 'service_role'` bypass. Also resolved the JWT-storage documentation tension FEAT-005 flagged (`api-design.md` §19 now correctly states the SDK's default `localStorage` persistence is the deliberate choice, not something avoided). Verified end-to-end against a local Supabase stack (`supabase start`): real signup → trigger creates the `profiles` row with `role = 'customer'`. 12 unit tests (`mapSupabaseError.test.ts`) + 4 unit tests (`profile.repository.test.ts`, fake Supabase client) + 3 integration tests (`RegisterForm.test.tsx`, real local Supabase) all pass.
- [x] `AUTH-002` / `AUTH-005` / Protected Routes — 2026-07-24. See `tasks/Sprint-02/AUTH-002-login-session-protected-routes.md`. Added `LoginSchema`, `useLogin`, `LoginForm` (generic `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` message; redirects back to `location.state.from` after success, falling back to home), real `LoginPage`. Mounted `AuthProvider` in `app/App.tsx` (wrapping `RouterProvider`) for app-wide session state from first render. Built `ProtectedRoute` (`features/authentication`) — a layout-route guard rendering nothing while the session resolves (no flash of "redirected to login" on refresh), redirecting to `/login` with `state.from` if unauthenticated, redirecting home if `allowedRoles` excludes the current role, otherwise rendering `<Outlet />`. Restructured `routes/routes.tsx`: `/dashboard`/`/favorites`/`/bookings`/`/profile` wrapped in `<ProtectedRoute />`; `/admin/*` wrapped in `<ProtectedRoute allowedRoles={['admin']} />`. `Header`/`MobileNavDrawer` now call `useAuth()` directly instead of receiving a static `authLinks` prop — the guest Login/Register buttons vs. authenticated name + Log out are genuinely reactive to session state; removed the now-meaningless `authLinks` prop and `AuthNavLink` type. 4 integration tests (`LoginForm.test.tsx`, `ProtectedRoute.test.tsx`) all pass against real local Supabase.
- [x] `AUTH-003` — Logout — 2026-07-24. See `tasks/Sprint-02/AUTH-003-logout.md`. Added `useLogout` and a "Log out" action in `Header`/`MobileNavDrawer`, visible whenever `useAuth()` reports a signed-in profile — reachable from every page via `AppLayout`'s persistent header. Logging out fires `onAuthStateChange`, which `AuthProvider` already subscribes to, so `profile` reactively becomes `null` and `ProtectedRoute` redirects away from any still-mounted protected page. No confirmation dialog — not required by the acceptance criteria.
- [x] `SYS-002` — Role-Based Authorization Verification — 2026-07-24. See `tasks/Sprint-02/SYS-002-role-based-authorization.md`. Wrote `profile.rls.test.ts` — 7 real integration tests against the local Supabase stack, using isolated per-actor Supabase clients (own `storageKey`) so multiple concurrently-authenticated identities can be exercised in one process; Agent/Moderator/Admin roles assigned via a `service_role` client (RLS-bypassing test fixture, the only path available before any admin UI exists). Proved: a Customer can read/update only their own profile; a Customer cannot update another Customer's profile; a non-admin cannot change their own role (`prevent_self_role_change` trigger); a Moderator can read every profile but a Customer cannot; an Admin can read/update any profile including changing roles; `roles` is publicly readable; a guest has zero access to `profiles`. Combined with `ProtectedRoute.test.tsx`'s new "Customer blocked from `/admin`, redirected home" case, this closes `roadmap.md` §6's full acceptance test — both the client-side redirect and the RLS-backed proof that it can't be bypassed by a direct query.

- [x] `AUTH-004` — Reset Forgotten Password — 2026-07-25. See `tasks/Sprint-02/AUTH-004-password-reset.md`. Added `RequestPasswordResetSchema`/`ResetPasswordSchema`, `useRequestPasswordReset`/`useResetPassword` hooks, `ForgotPasswordForm` (generic "check your email" confirmation shown regardless of whether the email is registered — never branches on what the request actually found) and `ResetPasswordForm` (new/confirm password fields, toast + redirect to `/login` on success). New `ForgotPasswordPage` replaces the `/forgot-password` placeholder; new `ResetPasswordPage` (`/reset-password`) gates on `useAuth()` — the emailed recovery link auto-establishes a temporary session via supabase-js's default `detectSessionInUrl` before this page renders the form; if no session exists once loading settles, an "invalid or expired link" message is shown instead. Both `authRepository.requestPasswordReset`/`resetPassword` already existed (built in `AUTH-001`) — this ticket added the missing Service-layer Zod validation, hooks, forms, pages, and route wiring around them. **Two small, real gaps found and fixed in the same change** (not new scope, just things the existing code hadn't hooked up yet): `PATHS.public` was missing a `resetPassword` entry even though the repository's `redirectTo` already pointed at `/reset-password`; and `mapSupabaseError.ts`'s `AUTH_ERROR_CODE_MAP` never mapped Supabase's `otp_expired` code to the already-declared `RESET_TOKEN_EXPIRED` `ErrorCode`, so an expired reset link would have shown the generic `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` message instead. Verified against the local Supabase stack: 6 new tests (3 `ForgotPasswordForm.test.tsx` — identical generic message for a real vs. a nonexistent email, plus an empty-email validation case; 2 `ResetPasswordForm.test.tsx` — a full reset that's confirmed by signing in with the *new* password after the old one is confirmed rejected, plus a mismatched-confirm-password validation case; 1 `mapSupabaseError.test.ts` case for `otp_expired`), bringing the suite to 41 tests, all passing. `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all pass clean.
- [x] `AUTH-006` — Manage Account Credentials — 2026-07-26. See `tasks/Sprint-02/AUTH-006-manage-account-credentials.md`. Scoped strictly to email/password per `user-stories.md`'s AUTH-006 acceptance criteria (editing name/phone is a separate future story, `CUST-003`, Sprint 5's Customer Dashboard — not touched here). Found a real gap first: `api-design.md` §5.8 (Update Profile) only documents `fullName`/`phone`/`avatarUrl`/`notificationPreferences` — there was no documented contract for changing email or password. Added §5.9 (Update Email) and §5.10 (Update Password) to `api-design.md` in the same change, before implementing against them. Built a new feature slice, `features/profile-management` (architecture.md §5 explicitly lists "Profile Management" as its own feature, distinct from `features/authentication` — this also makes it the second real feature slice, which is what the sibling-feature-import lint rule in **Technical Debt** needs to become testable): `credentials.repository.ts` (`getCurrentEmail`, `updateEmail`, `updatePassword`), `credentials.service.ts` (Zod validation), `useCurrentEmail`/`useUpdateEmail`/`useUpdatePassword` hooks, `UpdateEmailForm`/`UpdatePasswordForm` components, mounted on a new `ProfilePage` replacing the `/profile` placeholder. Email lives exclusively in `auth.users` (`database.md` §4.1), never in `profiles`, so `updateEmail` is a pure `supabase.auth.updateUser({ email })` call — no `profiles` write. There's no separate "verify current password" Supabase Auth API, so `updatePassword` re-authenticates for real via `supabase.auth.signInWithPassword()` with the current email + supplied current password before calling `updateUser({ password })`; a wrong current password surfaces as the existing generic `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` error, no new error code needed. Extracted a shared `passwordSchema` into `entities/user/user.schema.ts` (alongside the existing `fullNameSchema`/`phoneSchema`) and switched `RegisterSchema`/`ResetPasswordSchema` to use it — this was the rule's third duplication (Register, Reset Password, Update Password), the project's own stated extraction threshold (`coding-standards.md` §7). Verified against the local Supabase stack: 7 new unit tests (`credentials.repository.test.ts`, fake Supabase client — current-email lookup, email update, duplicate-email mapping, successful password update, wrong-current-password rejection that never calls `updateUser`, and the no-session guard) + 5 new integration tests (`UpdateEmailForm.test.tsx`, `UpdatePasswordForm.test.tsx` — a full password change confirmed by signing in with the *new* password after the old one is rejected, a wrong-current-password case that leaves the password unchanged, and validation-only cases), bringing the suite to 53 tests, all passing. `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all pass clean.

## Sprint 3 — Property Discovery ✅

- [x] Sibling-feature-import ESLint rule (Technical Debt closure) — 2026-07-27. Before adding the third feature slice, closed the gap ADR-022 deferred: `frontend/eslint.config.js`'s `features` element descriptor now captures the slice-folder name (`capture: ['feature']`), and the `features → features` allow rule requires `captured: { feature: '{{from.feature}}' }` instead of a blanket allow. Verified with a real throwaway cross-feature import file (blocked) and a same-feature import file (allowed), both deleted after. ADR-025 records this; ADR-022 marked superseded.
- [x] `property_discovery` migration (`supabase/migrations/20260727085311_property_discovery.sql`) — 2026-07-27. First schema addition since `auth_foundation`: `counties`/`locations`/`property_types`/`amenities` reference tables (+ idempotent seed matching `database.md` §12), `agencies`/`agents`/`properties`/`property_images`/`property_amenities`, the `property_status`/`verification_status` enums, `current_agency_id()` helper, every index from `database.md` §8, a simplified `enforce_verification_authority()` trigger (ahead of Sprint 7's sanctioning RPC — "RLS enabled, no exceptions" per `coding-standards.md` §12), and the **full** RLS Policy Summary for every table created (not just the guest-read subset this sprint's UI needs — correctly implementing what `database.md` already specifies for these tables in the one migration that creates them). **Two real, non-obvious bugs found and fixed during verification:** (1) `current_agency_id()` (a `language sql` function) was originally placed before the `agents` table it queries — unlike `plpgsql`, a `sql`-language function body is validated against the catalog at `CREATE FUNCTION` time, so this failed `supabase db reset` immediately; reordered after `agents`. (2) `database.md` §9's own prose called `agent_directory` a `security invoker` view, but an invoker-mode view would need the *caller's* RLS to already permit reading `profiles` — exactly the broad access the view exists to avoid granting guests; a real documentation bug, fixed to `security definer` (matching `current_role()`/`current_agency_id()`'s existing pattern) in the same change as `database.md` §9. Also added a new, previously-undocumented `property_ids_with_all_amenities()` SQL function for `api-design.md` §17's amenities AND-filter semantics, documented in `database.md` §9 in the same change. Verified directly against the local stack (not just a clean `db reset` exit): RLS enabled on all 9 new tables, seed counts match, every documented index present, the `rent_amount > 0` CHECK constraint rejects a negative insert, and a real `anon`-role query returns exactly the 7 guest-visible properties (rejected/archived correctly excluded).
- [x] `supabase/seed.sql` (new file, local-only) — 2026-07-27. Seeds 4 agent identities via direct `auth.users` inserts (`extensions.crypt()`-hashed passwords, firing `handle_new_user`) since `agents.profile_id` FKs through `profiles` to `auth.users` and this file can't call `supabase.auth.signUp()`; promotes them to `role = 'agent'` (temporarily disabling `prevent_self_role_change_trigger`, since a raw seed session has no `service_role` JWT context to satisfy its existing bypass — re-enabled immediately after). 2 agencies, 4 agents, 9 properties spanning verified+featured / verified / pending_verification / unverified / rejected / archived / available / reserved / occupied, plus images and amenities, so every documented UI state is reachable. Header comment states plainly this never runs against production (Sprint 9 replaces it with real reference data). Verified via `set role anon` queries directly against Postgres: exactly 7 of 9 properties visible, matching the RLS policy precisely.
- [x] `entities/property/` — 2026-07-27. `property.types.ts`/`property.mapper.ts`/`cursor.ts`/`property.repository.ts` (`list`/`listFeatured`, read-only this sprint — `getBySlug` deferred to Sprint 4)/`referenceData.repository.ts`/`PropertyCard.tsx`+`PropertyCardSkeleton.tsx`/`AvailabilityBadge.tsx`/`VerificationBadge.tsx`/`PriceDisplay.tsx`. `propertyRepository` deliberately lives in `entities/`, not `features/property-search/`, mirroring `profile.repository.ts`'s existing placement — both are cross-cutting reads later sprints' features will all need (ADR-026). The Favorite Button on `PropertyCard` renders permanently in its "unsaved" state (`aria-pressed={false}`, a neutral "coming soon" toast on click) rather than a fake toggle or omission, since FAV-001/002 are Sprint 5 scope but the Card's documented anatomy already includes it. Verified the `agent_directory` PostgREST embed actually resolves via `properties.agent_id` (a real risk flagged during planning, since the view has no declared FK) with a direct `curl` before writing any repository code around the assumption.
- [x] `features/property-search/` — 2026-07-27. A lenient `propertyFilters.schema.ts` (`.catch()` fallbacks — the URL query string is untrusted external input; malformed values degrade to undefined, never a rejected page load, a deliberate divergence from the strict write-path Zod convention) feeding `property.service.ts` → `entities/property`'s repository (Hook → Service → Repository). `useSearchFilters` wraps `react-router`'s `useSearchParams` as the single URL-synced source of truth (`NFR-SEARCH-004`); `useProperties` is the **first real `useInfiniteQuery` usage** in the project (TanStack Query has been wired since Sprint 1 but unused until now); `useFeaturedProperties`/`useReferenceData` round out the hooks. `SearchInput`/`SortSelect`/`FilterBar`/`FilterDrawer`/`ActiveFilterChips`/`EmptySearchResults` components, sharing one `FilterFields` internal component between the desktop bar and mobile drawer (`ui-guidelines.md` §13.3's own "same filter set, bar vs drawer").
- [x] Widgets — 2026-07-27. `SearchHero` (homepage-only, no feature hook — a plain submit-and-navigate box), `FeaturedListings` (self-contained, calls `useFeaturedProperties` itself), `PropertyGrid` (owns `useProperties`, renders loading/error/empty/success states explicitly plus an accessible "Load more" button rather than a silent scroll-trigger).
- [x] `HomePage`/`PropertiesPage` + routing — 2026-07-27. Replaced the `PlaceholderPage` entries for `PATHS.public.home`/`properties` in `routes/routes.tsx`; `propertyDetail` stays a placeholder (Sprint 4 scope).
- [x] New `shared/ui` primitives — 2026-07-27. `Card`/`Badge`/`Select`/`Checkbox`/`Skeleton` (shadcn CLI) plus a hand-built `empty-state.tsx` (icon → H4 → body → action, per `ui-guidelines.md` §19). Found and fixed a real token gap in the same change: `--color-success`/`--color-warning` had no `-foreground` pair — a programmatic WCAG contrast check showed white text fails AA (~3.2–3.8:1) against both in either theme, while `--color-foreground`'s light-mode value passes comfortably (4.7–8.3:1) in both, so both new tokens are a **fixed** dark value in both theme blocks (not a theme-flipping pair like `error`/`destructive`). `ui-guidelines.md` §21 updated to match.
- [x] Test suite — 2026-07-27. 33 new tests (86 total, up from 53): unit tests for `cursor.ts`, `property.mapper.ts`, the lenient filter schema (explicitly asserts the `.catch()` fallback behavior), and `listFeatured`'s error-normalization path (fake client); a real-Supabase RLS integration test (`property.rls.test.ts`, mirrors `profile.rls.test.ts`) proving the guest-visibility rule and `agent_directory`'s field whitelist directly against Postgres; a real-Supabase `PropertiesPage.test.tsx` that inserts 15 synthetic properties via a service-role client to force a genuine 20/22-row "Load more" boundary, proving cursor pagination has no duplicates/gaps, then deletes them in `afterAll`; and a `PropertyCard.test.tsx` component test. All 86 tests, lint, typecheck, and build pass clean.

## Sprint 4 — Property Details ✅ (fully closed 2026-07-29 — this header was left stale as "in progress" until this pass; see Active Sprint for the authoritative status)

- [x] `entities/property/property.repository.ts` — `getBySlug`/`listRelated` — 2026-07-28. Both reuse the existing `PROPERTY_COLUMNS` select verbatim. `getBySlug` mirrors `profileRepository.getById`'s not-found pattern exactly (`mapSupabaseError(error, { notFoundCode: 'PROPERTY_NOT_FOUND' })`). `listRelated` uses OR semantics (same county OR same type, not AND) so an uncommon location/type pairing still surfaces something — a new method, not a repurposed `.list()`, since that method's cursor/AND-filter shape is the wrong tool for a small fixed set with an exclusion.
- [x] `features/property-details/` — 2026-07-28. Mirrors `features/property-search`'s shape with a trivial passthrough Service (same precedent as Sprint 3's `listFeatured`) and deliberately **no Zod schema** — a `:slug` route param is an opaque path segment already matched by React Router, not an ambiguous filter object. `useProperty`/`useRelatedProperties` hooks.
- [x] Leaflet map — 2026-07-28. Installed `leaflet`/`@types/leaflet`, no `react-leaflet` (a single fixed marker doesn't need a second dependency's own React-19 peer-compat question). `PropertyMapCanvas.tsx` is the only file importing `leaflet`; `PropertyMap.tsx` wraps it in `React.lazy`/`Suspense` so it's kept out of the eager bundle — confirmed via a real build that it's its own ~150KB chunk, separate from the shared bundle. "Get Directions" is a plain `google.com/maps/dir` link (no `geo:` URI — it only resolves reliably on Android; this works identically everywhere via each platform's own app links).
- [x] `PropertyGallery`/`ImageGalleryViewer` — 2026-07-28. Hand-built on the existing `Sheet`/Radix Dialog primitive (free focus-trap/`Esc`-close) rather than adding a carousel dependency. Desktop hero + 2×2 grid with a "+N photos" overlay; mobile CSS scroll-snap row (not a JS carousel) with visible prev/next buttons. Full-screen viewer: keyboard arrow-key navigation and an `aria-live` region announcing each image's `alt_text`, both confirmed working in a real browser (`ArrowRight` advanced the counter, `Escape` closed it).
- [x] `AmenitiesGrid` + `amenityIcons.ts` — 2026-07-28. A small hand-built icon-name → Lucide-component map for the ~10 seeded amenity icons (not `lucide-react`'s dynamic-import mechanism — a fixed curated catalogue doesn't need per-icon async chunks), with a `HelpCircle` fallback for any future unmapped icon. `AmenitiesGrid` is presentational only (takes `available`/`all` as props, diffs internally) — it doesn't fetch data itself, since `entities/` can't import `features/`; `PropertyDetailPage` calls the already-existing `useAmenities()` hook and passes the catalogue down.
- [x] `AgentCard`, `PriceBreakdown`, `ShareButton`; `FavoriteButton` extracted — 2026-07-28. `AgentCard` deliberately doesn't fetch agency name/logo — not part of `PROP-005`'s actual acceptance criteria, only its `ui-guidelines.md` visual anatomy; noted in the component rather than silently added (scope creep) or silently dropped. `PriceBreakdown` is a new component, not a mode-flag on the existing card-only `PriceDisplay` — different anatomies. `ShareButton`: Web Share API with a clipboard-copy + toast fallback, a cancelled share sheet (`AbortError`) silently ignored, never shown as an error. `FavoriteButton` was private/inline inside `PropertyCard.tsx`; extracted to its own file since `FAV-001` itself requires it on both listing cards and the detail page — required reuse, not speculative.
- [x] `ViewingCTA` — 2026-07-28. Always disabled this sprint (booking doesn't exist yet, independent of availability/guest state — implementing half of `ui-guidelines.md` §12.14's guest-redirect rule would be less honest than a single generic "coming soon" state), sticky-bottom on mobile per already-decided layout, tagged `// TODO(VIEW-001, Sprint 5)`.
- [x] `widgets/related-properties/RelatedProperties.tsx` — 2026-07-28. Mirrors `FeaturedListings`' self-contained shape exactly, including its "render `null` on error/empty" handling for a secondary section.
- [x] `pages/PropertyDetailPage.tsx` + routing — 2026-07-28. Composes everything in `ui-guidelines.md` §12.4's exact documented order; lazy-routed the same way every other Sprint 3 page is (imported directly, never through the `@/pages` barrel). Replaces the `propertyDetail` placeholder.
- [x] Test suite — 2026-07-28. 16 new tests (102 total, up from 86): a fake-client unit test for `getBySlug`'s not-found mapping; 3 unit tests for the icon-fallback map; 4 new real-Supabase RLS integration cases extending `property.rls.test.ts` (`getBySlug`/`listRelated` both correctly exclude rejected/archived fixture properties); component tests for `FavoriteButton`/`ShareButton`/`ViewingCTA` (one real bug found while writing `ShareButton`'s test: `userEvent.click` interfered with a stubbed `navigator.clipboard` in this jsdom setup for reasons not fully root-caused — worked around with `fireEvent.click`, documented inline); and a real-Supabase `PropertyDetailPage.test.tsx` proving every field renders from real seeded data plus a not-found case. All 102 tests, lint, typecheck, and build pass clean.

## Sprint 5 — Customer Experience ✅ (fully closed 2026-07-29 — this header was left stale as "in progress" until this pass; see Active Sprint for the authoritative status)

- [x] `supabase/migrations/20260729090000_customer_experience.sql` — 2026-07-29. `viewing_status` enum, `favorites` (composite PK), `viewing_requests` (denormalized `agent_id` snapshot, `requested_date >= current_date` CHECK), `current_agent_id()` helper, `prevent_booking_unavailable_property()` trigger (`RH001` errcode), full RLS Policy Summary for both tables (agent SELECT/UPDATE grant shipped ahead of Sprint 6's UI, same precedent as Sprint 3), plus two new `properties` SELECT policies so a favorited/booked property stays visible to its own customer once archived/rejected. **Two real bugs found and fixed via the RLS integration test suite, not assumed away:** the new `properties` policies initially applied to `anon` too (no `to authenticated` clause), and evaluating their subquery against `favorites`/`viewing_requests` — tables `anon` has zero grant on — broke **every** guest query against `properties` with a blanket 42501; and `viewing_requests` was missing a `DELETE` grant for `authenticated`, making the `viewing_requests_manage_admin` policy's admin `for all` unreachable. Both fixed in the same migration file before merge (not yet pushed anywhere, so editing in place is correct per `database.md` §13). Verified directly: `supabase db reset` clean, `\d` output confirms every table/policy/trigger/index, and the full RLS test suite (27 tests across `property.rls.test.ts`/`favorites.rls.test.ts`/`viewing-request.rls.test.ts`) passes.
- [x] `entities/viewing-request/` — 2026-07-29. New slice: types/mapper/repository (`create`/`cancel`/`listForCustomer` only this sprint — Sprint 6 extends the same interface in place), `ViewingStatusBadge`, `BookingCard` (customer-scoped cancel action only; shaped so Sprint 6 can add agent actions without a rewrite). Repository placed in `entities/`, not `features/`, per new **ADR-028** — `viewing_requests` reads/mutations are genuinely cross-cutting (Sprint 6's `BOOK-*` needs the same table), unlike `favorites` (see below).
- [x] `features/favorites/` — 2026-07-29. Repository placed *here* instead (ADR-028's opposite case — nothing outside this feature needs favorites data). `save`/`remove`/`list`/`listIds` (the last one new, undocumented in `api-design.md` originally — added since `list()`'s full expanded shape is too heavy to call from every card just to check saved-state). `useFavoriteIds`/`useToggleFavorite`/`useFavorites` hooks; `useToggleFavorite` is the single sanctioned place guest-redirect and optimistic-update logic live, shared by every `PropertyCard`-rendering call site via one deduped TanStack Query key.
- [x] `features/viewing-requests/` — 2026-07-29. Zod schemas (`createViewingRequest`/`cancelViewingRequest`, native `<input type="date"/"time">` — no picker library, `ui-guidelines.md` is silent on one and every `VIEW-002` criterion is satisfiable natively), `viewing-request.service.ts` (Service-layer availability pre-flight check before the DB trigger backstop), hooks (`useCreateViewingRequest`/`useCancelViewingRequest`/`useViewingRequests`/`useViewingRequestsRealtime` — the realtime subscription deliberately separated from the data hook to avoid duplicate `postgres_changes` channels when a page calls the data hook more than once), `BookingRequestDialog`/`CancelBookingDialog` (new `shared/ui/dialog.tsx` — a real gap found during planning, `ui-guidelines.md` §11.10 names Dialog for exactly these two interactions but only `sheet.tsx` existed), `ViewingRequestList` (owns cancel-dialog wiring internally so `DashboardPage`/`BookingsPage` don't duplicate it).
- [x] `entities/property/FavoriteButton.tsx`/`ViewingCTA.tsx` rewritten; `PropertyCard.tsx` extended — 2026-07-29. Both go from Sprint 3/4's permanent stub state to real controlled components — `entities/` can't import `features/` (`eslint.config.js`'s boundary rule), so every interactive piece takes props (`isSaved`/`onToggle`, `availabilityStatus`/`onBook`) and the actual feature-hook wiring happens in whichever widget/page renders them, mirroring `AmenitiesGrid`'s existing presentational-only precedent. `PropertyCard` gained a required `favorite` prop bundle and an "Archived" badge (`FAV-003`'s "unavailable or archived saved properties are clearly marked").
- [x] `features/profile-management/` extended (`CUST-003`/`004`) — 2026-07-29. `UpdateProfileForm`/`NotificationPreferencesForm`, both wired directly onto `profileRepository.update()`, which already fully supported `fullName`/`phone`/`notificationPreferences` since an earlier sprint — no repository changes needed. `CUST-004`'s "not fully disable-able, only their delivery channel" AC is honestly read as a checked-and-disabled toggle (no real delivery-channel/notification pipeline exists yet to make a channel selector meaningful) rather than over-built.
- [x] Widgets wired for favorites — 2026-07-29. `PropertyGrid`/`FeaturedListings`/`RelatedProperties` each call `useFavoriteIds`/`useToggleFavorite` and pass the resulting prop bundle into every `PropertyCard`.
- [x] `pages/FavoritesPage.tsx`/`DashboardPage.tsx`/`BookingsPage.tsx` (new) + `ProfilePage.tsx`/`PropertyDetailPage.tsx` (extended) + routing — 2026-07-29. All three new routes replace their `PlaceholderPage` placeholders in `routes/routes.tsx`, same lazy-import pattern as every other page. `PropertyDetailPage` wires the real `FavoriteButton`/`ViewingCTA`/`BookingRequestDialog` (guest click → `/login`; customer click → dialog).
- [x] Test suite — 2026-07-29. 46 net new tests (148 total, up from 102 — recounted precisely via a direct per-file `grep`, not assumed): unit (`favorites.repository.test.ts`, `viewing-request.repository.test.ts`, 4 new Zod schema files, a `mapSupabaseError` `RH001` case), two new real-Supabase RLS suites (`favorites.rls.test.ts` 5 tests, `viewing-request.rls.test.ts` 11 tests — together proving ownership isolation, the archived-property carve-out, the booking trigger, the CHECK constraint, and the agent RLS grant with no agent UI built yet), rewritten `FavoriteButton`/`PropertyCard`/`ViewingCTA` component tests, and a real-Supabase `FavoritesPage.test.tsx` proving the full save→list→remove loop end to end (FAV-002's "no page reload" requirement). Also fixed two pre-existing test files (`PropertiesPage.test.tsx`, `PropertyDetailPage.test.tsx`) that crashed outright (not flaked) because they render components that now call `useAuth()`/`useFavoriteIds()` without an `AuthProvider` ancestor — a real regression from this sprint's own wiring, not pre-existing flakiness; `PropertyDetailPage.test.tsx`'s stale "Viewing CTA, disabled" assertion was also updated to the now-correct enabled state for an available property. `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean.
- [x] Docs — 2026-07-29. `database.md` §9: added `current_agent_id()`, the two new `properties` policies (with the anon-permission-pitfall rationale as a general rule for future policies), and the `RH001` errcode convention. `api-design.md`: new §7.4 (`listIds()`), §8.3's signature widened to an options object with `sort`, §15.3 gained the `RH001` mechanism note and the `cancel()` → `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` normalization note. `decisions.md`: new **ADR-028** (repository-placement split between `entities/viewing-request` and `features/favorites`, extending ADR-026's precedent).
- [x] Commit/push to `main` — 2026-07-29, as two commits: `f4218d3` (the Sprint 4 `useProperty.ts` fix) and `9caac20` (all of Sprint 5). The first push accidentally bundled both under a single undersized commit message (`29eb142`) — caught, and since it was already on `origin/main`, corrected via `git reset --soft` to before it, re-split into the two commits above, and `git push --force-with-lease` to replace it. Verified the resulting tree is byte-identical to the original bundled commit (`git diff 29eb142 HEAD` produces no output) — content was never at risk, only the commit boundary/message. Both commits authored solely by the developer, no co-author trailer, per explicit instruction.
- [x] Deployed — 2026-07-29, automatically via Vercel's push-triggered CI/CD. Confirmed via matching production asset hash (`index-DoZp5EFU.js`).
- [x] Migration pushed to remote Supabase — 2026-07-29. Attempting `supabase db push` directly was blocked by this session's own auto-mode safety classifier (a guard on the command itself, independent of the developer's explicit authorization); the developer ran it directly. Verified against the remote (not just `supabase migration list`'s clean sync): a guest `INSERT` into `favorites` correctly fails with `42501` (RLS policy violation), and a guest `INSERT` into `viewing_requests` correctly fails via the `prevent_booking_unavailable_property()` trigger itself, returning the real `RH001` errcode live from production — both RLS and the trigger confirmed genuinely active on remote, not just present in the schema. **Sprint 5 is now fully closed** — production's frontend and database are back in sync.

Worked around by testing `RescheduleBookingDialog`/`CancelBookingDialog` directly with `open` controlled as a prop (two new test files) instead of driving the DropdownMenu transition, and by narrowing `AgentBookingQueue.test.tsx`'s BOOK-003/004 coverage to the menu's available actions. See **Technical Debt** for a second, unrelated finding from this pass (a pre-existing cross-file test-pollution gap between `property.rls.test.ts` and `PropertiesPage.test.tsx`, surfaced only by a truly complete `npm run test` run).
- [x] The manual real-browser walkthrough with two seeded agents, commit/push/migrate/deploy — all done 2026-07-30. **Sprint 6 is fully closed.**

| ~~`AgentDashboardOverviewPage.test.tsx`'s "counts only this agent's own agency properties and own assigned viewing requests" test failed deterministically~~ **Root-caused and fixed, 2026-08-02.** Not a counting bug at all: the test's `viewing_requests` fixture insert used a **hardcoded** `requested_date: '2026-08-01'` for its "completed" row. `viewing_requests_requested_date_check` (`database.md` §9) requires `requested_date >= current_date`, and once the real calendar date passed 2026-08-01, that hardcoded value started violating the constraint — failing the entire two-row batch insert (Postgres rolls back the whole `INSERT` on one row's constraint violation), silently, since the test never checked the insert's `error`. Both `pendingViewings` and `completedViewings` showed 0 because *neither* row was ever actually persisted — the dashboard's own repository/query logic was correct the whole time, confirmed by calling it directly with a hand-built fixture (returned the right counts immediately). Diagnosed via a throwaway RTL+direct-repository comparison test (not by reading code and guessing), which isolated the failure to the `.insert()` call itself once its error was surfaced. Fixed: the fixture now uses a date computed relative to "now" (`Date.now() + 7 days`) instead of a fixed calendar date, plus the insert's `error` is now checked and thrown — so a future occurrence of this bug class fails loudly with the real Postgres error instead of masquerading as a wrong UI count. Verified stable across 4 consecutive isolated runs. **This also means the "full-suite contention flakiness" this exact test has been dismissed as since Sprint 6/7 was likely this same rotting-date bug all along**, not contention — worth remembering next time a "flaky" integration test involves hardcoded dates. | — (resolved) | — | Closed 2026-08-02. |

| ~~`frontend/analyze-chunk.cjs`/`frontend/check-contrast.cjs` remain untracked, uncommitted debug scripts, developer hasn't chosen commit-as-tooling vs. delete~~ **Resolved 2026-08-05 — kept as permanent tooling, wired as `npm run analyze:chunk`/`npm run check:contrast`.** A real, active bug was found and fixed while deciding, not just while tidying: `check-contrast.cjs` hardcoded a snapshot of the design tokens inline, and that snapshot had already drifted from reality — it still had `secondary-foreground: #FFFFFF`, the *pre*-Sprint-8-fix value, while `styles/index.css` has held the real, corrected `#0f172a` since that fix shipped. Running the stale script would have silently reported a false FAIL forever. Rewritten to parse `src/styles/index.css`'s `@theme` block directly instead of hardcoding a duplicate — confirmed correct by re-running it: `secondary-foreground` now reports exactly `4.74`, matching Sprint 8's own documented fix number. (One pre-existing, unrelated FAIL the script also reports — `subtle-foreground` on `background`, 2.45 against a 3.0 threshold — is unchanged from before this fix and was already implicitly accepted by Sprint 8, per its own notes only naming `secondary-foreground` and the skip-link as real gaps; not touched, not this task's scope.) `analyze-chunk.cjs` was verified working as-is against a real `ANALYZE=1` build, then given a proper usage header, a friendly "stats.json not found" message, and an "available chunks" listing when a name doesn't match (it previously required knowing the exact `assets/`-prefixed, hash-suffixed chunk name with no way to discover it). Neither script is a CI gate — both stay on-demand diagnostics, exactly as they always were. | — (resolved) | — | Closed 2026-08-05. |

| ~~`claude-in-chrome` failed to connect at all, 2026-08-05~~ **Partially resolved, same date, later session.** Reconnected after the developer checked/fixed the extension on their end. Two narrower issues remained even once connected, worth tracking separately: (1) `resize_window` still doesn't work — confirmed again, and this time root-caused further: even the developer manually resizing their own visible Chrome window didn't change what the automated tab reported, meaning the extension was controlling a *different* window than the one visible to the developer. Worked around by opening a fresh tab via `tabs_create_mcp` (lands in the developer's real window) and having them resize that specific window/tab — this worked, confirmed via `window.innerWidth`. (2) Mouse-click (`computer` tool) and screenshot (`Page.captureScreenshot`) both threw frequent `CDP sendCommand ... timed out after 30000ms` errors throughout the mobile QA pass below — sometimes the underlying action still landed (confirmed via follow-up state checks), sometimes it didn't. Worked around for the QA pass by using `javascript_tool` directly: native `<input>` value setters + `form.requestSubmit()` for forms, `element.click()` for plain buttons/links — reliable for both, but does **not** work for Radix-driven pointer-event interactions (e.g. the account-menu dropdown), so those specific interactions still went untested. | Medium — real device/browser testing remains possible via these workarounds, but every session needing it currently has to rediscover and re-apply them rather than the tools just working. | Medium | Open. `resize_window` and click/screenshot CDP reliability both need a real fix or replacement, not another one-off workaround next time.

| Leftover RLS/adversarial-test rows ("RLS Test Property," "Adversarial Test Property," and similar) are visible in the **live** Agent Properties list and Moderator Verification Queue, found during the 2026-08-05 mobile QA pass. The durable fix is `afterAll` cleanup in the RLS/adversarial test files that create these rows (several already have this pattern established, e.g. `verification.rls.test.ts`'s own tracked-id cleanup) — a `supabase db reset` before any real demo/screenshot session is the immediate workaround, not a permanent fix. |

**2026-07-24 — Vercel connection completed; production deployment verified live.** The developer connected the GitHub repository to Vercel (outside this session) and shared the production URL, `https://rental-hunt.vercel.app/`. **2026-07-27 — Production registration bug found and fixed: remote Supabase Auth "Confirm email" was on.** The developer reported registration on `https://rental-hunt.vercel.app/` failing with `"Registration succeeded but no session was returned"` — this is `auth.repository.ts`'s explicit guard firing when `supabase.auth.signUp()` returns a `user` but `session: null`. Root cause: `supabase/config.toml` sets `enable_confirmations = false` for the local stack (matching `AUTH-001`'s "signed in immediately" requirement), but `supabase db push` never pushes Auth service settings, only schema/migrations — the remote project had been left on Supabase Cloud's default, "Confirm email" enabled, so `signUp()` withheld the session until email confirmation. This had never surfaced before because all 53 automated tests and every prior verification ran against the local stack, where the setting already matched what the docs require. Considered using `supabase config push` to sync the whole `config.toml` to remote, but rejected it — `config.toml`'s `site_url`/`additional_redirect_urls` are still local dev values (`http://127.0.0.1:3000`), and pushing them would have overwritten production's real redirect URLs, likely breaking password-reset/email-change links. Instead, the developer disabled "Confirm email" directly in the Supabase Dashboard (Authentication → Providers → Email) — a scoped, zero-collateral-risk fix. Verified fixed two ways: a direct `POST /auth/v1/signup` against the production endpoint (bypassing the frontend, to isolate the Auth-service setting from the app code) returned a real session immediately, and a follow-up authenticated `GET /rest/v1/profiles` with that session's token confirmed the `handle_new_user` trigger had created the row with `role: "customer"`; the developer separately confirmed the full flow in a real browser against production. Logged as **Technical Debt** (above) since the underlying local/remote Auth-config parity gap — not just this one setting — is still open. No application code changed; this was an infrastructure/configuration fix.

Committed as `4b673a9` and pushed to `origin/main` at the developer's request. `supabase db push` then applied the migration to the remote Supabase project (ref `oqcaythakxgcpgfsqifu`) — verified directly via REST, not just a clean CLI exit: `properties`/`agent_directory`/`property_ids_with_all_amenities()` are all reachable, and the in-migration reference-data seed (4 counties, 8 property types, 10 amenities) loaded correctly. The remote database has zero property rows (`supabase/seed.sql`'s sample fixtures are local-only by design), so a production real-browser pass right now would mostly exercise the empty-search-results state rather than the full populated experience — flagged in **Next Recommended Action**, not silently assumed equivalent to the local verification pass.

**2026-07-28 — Sprint 4 committed/pushed/deployed; a real production-only bug found and fixed.** The developer committed and pushed Sprint 4 themselves (`59e16bd` — the commit message got garbled, concatenating the previous commit's body text in; `git show --stat` confirmed all 36 expected files present with correct line counts, so only the message text is affected, not the content). Deployment confirmed live via matching production asset hashes. The mandatory production real-browser pass then surfaced a real, reproducible bug: navigating to `2br-apartment-kilimani-a1` (seeded locally, absent from the remote database, which has zero property rows by design) showed the generic error message instead of the specific "could not be found" one. Diagnosed by importing the actual deployed production JS chunks directly in the browser (via dynamic `import()` of the real hashed chunk URLs) and calling the real, already-running singletons — the production Supabase client, `mapSupabaseError`, and `isAppError` — against the real PostgREST 406 response: all three proved the `PROPERTY_NOT_FOUND` classification was correct from the very first failed attempt (confirmed by walking the live React fiber tree and reading TanStack Query's internal `failureReason`). The actual defect: `useProperty` had no `retry` override, so a deterministic 404 was retried like a transient network error; in the gap between attempts, `isLoading`/`isError` are both `false` and `error` is still `null`, which `PropertyDetailPage`'s `isError || !property` check misreads as "still no data," falling through to the generic branch. Fixed by adding a `retry` predicate to `useProperty` that fails fast on `PROPERTY_NOT_FOUND` specifically, leaving default retry behavior for genuinely transient errors. Verified: `PropertyDetailPage.test.tsx` passes, lint (0 errors)/typecheck/build all clean. Noted 5 pre-existing, unrelated integration-test failures (`LoginForm`, `RegisterForm`, `PropertiesPage`, `UpdateEmailForm`) consistent with this project's already-documented local-Supabase cold-start/contention flakiness — not caused by this change. **Not yet committed, pushed, or deployed.**

**2026-07-29 — Sprint 5 (Customer Experience) planned and implemented.** Given a plan-mode request for Sprint 5, read `roadmap.md` §9, the `FAV-*`/`VIEW-*`/`CUST-*` stories, and the relevant `database.md`/`api-design.md`/`ui-guidelines.md` sections, then ran a Plan subagent (resumed once after it hit a session limit mid-run) for a concrete file-level plan before writing code. The plan itself surfaced three real things the initial brief hadn't anticipated, each resolved and documented rather than silently picked one way: (1) an RLS gap where a favorited/booked archived property would be invisible to the customer who favorited it — fixed with two new, carefully role-scoped `properties` policies; (2) no `Dialog` primitive existed yet (only `Sheet`), needed for the booking form and cancel-confirmation per `ui-guidelines.md` §11.10; (3) `listForCustomer`'s documented signature couldn't serve all three call sites' different orderings without an options-object change. Implemented in order: the migration (enum, tables, trigger with a dedicated `RH001` errcode, full RLS including the agent grant a sprint ahead of its UI, per Sprint 3's own precedent), `entities/viewing-request` (repository placement decided via new **ADR-028**, extending ADR-026's reasoning to a second entity landing on the opposite side of the same test), `features/favorites`/`features/viewing-requests`, the `FavoriteButton`/`ViewingCTA` rewrite from permanent stubs into controlled components (the FSD boundary forces every side effect up into whichever widget/page renders them), three new pages, and `ProfilePage`'s `CUST-003`/`004` extension (built entirely on an already-existing, previously-unused-for-this-purpose `profileRepository.update()` method). **Two real bugs were found and fixed via the test suite, not assumed away:** the new `properties` policies initially applied to `anon` (no `to authenticated` clause), and evaluating their subquery against tables `anon` has zero grant on broke *every* guest property query — a serious regression that `property.rls.test.ts` caught by actually regressing, not something reasoned about in the abstract; and `viewing_requests` was missing an admin `DELETE` grant. Also found and fixed two pre-existing test files (`PropertiesPage.test.tsx`, `PropertyDetailPage.test.tsx`) that started crashing (not flaking) once the components they render gained a `useAuth()`/`useFavoriteIds()` dependency with no `AuthProvider` ancestor in the test harness — a real regression from this sprint's own wiring, caught and fixed in the same pass rather than left for later. 46 net new tests (148 total, recounted precisely). `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean. Updated `database.md` §9 (new helper function, new policies, the anon-permission pitfall as a general rule, the `RH001` convention), `api-design.md` (§7.4, §8.3, §15.3), and `decisions.md` (ADR-028) in the same change as the code each documents. **Did not commit, push, push the migration, or deploy** — per this project's established pattern, those are separate, later, explicitly-requested steps; a real-browser manual pass at 375px is also still outstanding.

**2026-07-30 — Sprint 6's manual two-agent browser walkthrough completed; a real cross-agency data leak found and fixed.** Before it could run at all, a pre-existing `seed.sql` bug blocked logging in as *any* seeded user (agent or customer, not Sprint-6-specific): the raw `auth.users` inserts left `confirmation_token`/`recovery_token`/`email_change_token_new`/`email_change` as `NULL` (no column default), which GoTrue's driver can't scan on a *login* request specifically (`"Scan error on column index 3... converting NULL to string is unsupported"`) — never surfaced before because every prior verification signed a fresh user up through the real app flow instead of logging in with a directly-seeded row. Fixed with explicit `''` on all four columns. Separately, this session's many consecutive `supabase db reset`s had left Kong with a stale route to the Auth container (`connect() failed... Host is unreachable`) — fixed with a full `supabase stop && start`. With both fixed, logged in via `claude-in-chrome` as `agent2.seed@rentalhunt.test` (Nairobi Homes) and `agent4.seed@rentalhunt.test` (Kiambu Estates) and found a real bug immediately: agent2's own Properties/Dashboard/Analytics all showed 7 properties instead of 6 — one of Kiambu's guest-visible properties was leaking into Nairobi Homes' own management views. Root cause: `properties`' guest-visibility RLS policy has no role restriction and OR-combines with the agent-specific "own agency" policy, so an unscoped agent query returns "own agency (full) ∪ every other agency's guest-visible rows" — correct for public browsing, wrong for an agency-scoped management screen. Confirmed via a direct `UPDATE` attempt that this was read-only, not a write vulnerability (0 rows affected, the dedicated agent-`UPDATE`-own-agency policy was unaffected). Fixed by adding an explicit `agency_id` filter to `propertyRepository.listForAgent()`, `agentDashboardRepository.getSummary()`, and `agentAnalyticsRepository.listPropertyAnalytics()` (threaded through their Services/hooks via `getCurrentAgent()`), and documented as a new general rule in `database.md` §9. Re-verified live in the browser: both agents now see exactly their own agency's properties. Beyond the bug hunt, confirmed working end-to-end through the real UI: create listing, submit for verification, image upload to the real Storage bucket, inline availability toggle, and confirming a pending viewing request with the customer's name/phone correctly shown. A narrower version of the same root cause remained in `propertyRepository.getById()` (agent edit-form fetch) — logged as Technical Debt, not fixed in that same pass. `.env.local` was temporarily pointed at the local stack for this pass and reverted back to remote afterward, per established convention.

**2026-07-30 — `getById()`'s agency-scoping gap closed, at the developer's explicit request.** Added `agencyId` as a required second parameter (`getById(id, agencyId)`), with its own `.eq('agency_id', ...)` query rather than reusing the shared `fetchById()` helper (which stays as-is for `create`/`update`/`archive`/`updateAvailability`/`submitForVerification` — those are already correctly scoped by RLS's agent-`UPDATE`-own-agency policy on the write side, so they didn't need it). Also found and fixed a second, smaller gap in the same pass: `useAgentProperty`'s hook was calling `propertyRepository.getById()` directly, skipping the Service layer entirely — added `agentPropertyService.getById()` (resolves `getCurrentAgent()`, matching `listForAgent`'s already-fixed pattern) and pointed the hook at it instead, restoring the Hook → Service → Repository chain. New RLS integration test proves the exact scenario found via manual testing — a `verified` (guest-visible) property belonging to a different agency — is reachable via an unscoped query and correctly excluded once the `agency_id` filter is added. `npm run lint`/`typecheck` clean; the full `property.rls.test.ts` suite (16 tests) and `features/agent-properties`'s suite (13 tests) pass against a freshly reset local stack.

**2026-07-30 — Sprint 6's deferred component/page-level tests written; a genuine jsdom/Radix environment bug found and worked around; a pre-existing cross-file test-pollution gap discovered.** Six new test files: `PropertyForm.test.tsx`, `ImageManager.test.tsx`, `AgentBookingQueue.test.tsx`, `PropertyAnalyticsChart.test.tsx` + `PropertyAnalyticsTable.test.tsx`, `AgentDashboardOverviewPage.test.tsx` (real-Supabase), `AgentPropertyFormPage.test.tsx` (real-Supabase, its third test doubling as a page-level proof of the `getById` agency-scoping fix above). `src/test/setup.ts` gained a `ResizeObserver` polyfill — Radix `Select` and recharts' `ResponsiveContainer` are the first things in this project to need one, and jsdom doesn't provide it.

While writing `AgentBookingQueue.test.tsx`, driving the real DropdownMenuItem-select → Dialog-open transition (the actual BOOK-003/004 Reschedule/Cancel pattern) caused a genuine, non-terminating `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` — a recursive `focus()` loop between jsdom and `@radix-ui/react-focus-scope`. Confirmed via an isolated throwaway repro (outside any test file, since deleted) that this reproduces regardless of `userEvent` vs. `fireEvent`, ruling out a test-authoring mistake; also confirmed a plain already-open `Dialog` mounts fine standalone, isolating the trigger to specifically the DropdownMenu-closing/Dialog-opening handoff. Treated as an environment limitation, not a real app bug (already relied on in production, manually verified in-browser earlier this sprint) — worked around by testing `RescheduleBookingDialog`/`CancelBookingDialog` directly via two new test files (`open` controlled as a prop, no DropdownMenu involved), and narrowing `AgentBookingQueue.test.tsx` itself to assert the menu offers the right actions without completing the transition. (Two stuck `vitest` worker processes from this investigation had to be killed manually via PowerShell after `TaskStop` didn't terminate the underlying Windows node children — the session's dev server, running throughout, was confirmed untouched both times.)

A full, unscoped `npm run test` (not a sub-path run) was then run twice to verify nothing regressed, surfacing a real, pre-existing issue unrelated to any code in this pass: `property.rls.test.ts`'s own fixtures default `verification_status: 'verified'` (guest-visible), inflating `PropertiesPage.test.tsx`'s exact global count assertion when both run in the same parallel worker batch. This session's two new page-test files were first found to independently contribute the same way and were fixed (fixtures switched to `'rejected'`, the one status excluded from guest visibility) — the discrepancy still reproduced afterward from `property.rls.test.ts` alone, confirming it predates this pass. Logged as new Technical Debt rather than fixed here (out of scope — would mean editing `property.rls.test.ts`, not one of this pass's own files). A Storage RLS test and `PropertyAnalyticsChart.test.tsx`'s lazy-load assertion each timed out once under full parallel load but passed cleanly in isolation — the same known full-suite contention flakiness already documented from earlier sprints, not new.

Final state: `npm run lint` (0 errors, 20 pre-existing warnings), `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run build` all clean; every new test passes reliably (confirmed individually and in feature-scoped groupings) against a freshly reset local stack. Committed as `ffeb7cd` and pushed to `origin/main`.

**2026-07-30 — The `property.rls.test.ts`/`PropertiesPage.test.tsx` test-pollution debt closed, at the developer's explicit request ("fix test then push to db").** Two of `property.rls.test.ts`'s three guest-visible-by-default fixtures were switched to `'rejected'` (the RPC-submission test and the Storage RLS test, neither of which actually needed guest visibility — only the leak-reproduction test does, and was left unchanged). `PropertiesPage.test.tsx`'s pagination assertion was hardened from an exact `toBe(22)` to `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(22)` plus an explicit check that all 15 of its own synthetic fixture slugs are present, since pinning an exact count on a table other test files can concurrently write to was the actual fragility, not any one file's fixture choice. **2026-07-31 — Sprint 7 (Administration) planned and implemented; not yet closed.** Given a request to kick off Sprint 7, read `roadmap.md` §11, confirmed no dedicated `ADMIN-*`/`MOD-*` story IDs exist in `user-stories.md` (this sprint is scoped by the roadmap + `api-design.md` §9 directly), and ran two Explore passes plus a Plan-agent pass (approved) before writing code — the plan surfaced several real findings ahead of implementation worth recording: `profiles`/`agencies`/`properties` already had full Admin RLS from Sprints 2/3 (nothing to add there); `prevent_self_role_change_trigger` already correctly lets an admin change *another* user's role (`current_role()` resolves the acting caller, not the target row); and `activity_logs` would be permanently empty without also building the five trigger-based event types `database.md` §11 already specified, so those were built now rather than left for a hypothetical later pass.
