new `entities/agency`/`entities/property-verification` slices; `entities/property.getByIdAdmin()`; six new feature slices (`admin-verification`/`admin-dashboard`/`admin-analytics`/`admin-users`/`admin-agencies`/`admin-activity-log`), each repository placed per new **ADR-030** (a mechanical application of ADR-026/028's "2+ real consumers" test to five decisions at once); `widgets/admin-dashboard-layout`; six new pages; routing/`paths.constants.ts` extended; `shared/lib/logger.ts` + `shared/ui/route-error-boundary.tsx` (closing roadmap §11's "System Monitoring" bullet, wired into all three top-level layouts); a new `useAgentPropertyVerificationRealtime()` hook and `VerificationStatusPanel` extension so the agent's own side genuinely updates "without a manual refresh," not just the moderator's. **One real bug caught and fixed before anything shipped, not by a test:** an early draft of `set_property_verification()` also inserted a redundant `activity_logs` row on every verification change — rereading `database.md` §11's own Tracked Events table while writing this update surfaced that it had already explicitly decided against this ("the one MVP event type that bypasses activity_logs entirely"); removed, and the RLS test that had been asserting the (wrong) row was rewritten to assert its absence. **Two real test-environment issues found and fixed during the testing pass:** mounting the new Realtime hook inside `AgentPropertyFormPage` broke that page's own real-Supabase integration test with an uncaught `WebSocket.dispatchEvent` exception during jsdom teardown (an undici/jsdom compatibility quirk, the same class of genuine environment limitation Sprint 6 hit once for Radix's focus-scope) — fixed by mocking the hook in that one test file, mirroring Sprint 6's own `AgentBookingQueue.test.tsx` narrowing precedent; and `verification.rls.test.ts`'s own `verified`-status fixtures inflated `property.rls.test.ts`'s exact "guest sees exactly 7 properties" count when both ran in the same parallel worker batch — the identical test-pollution class Sprint 6 already found and fixed once for `PropertiesPage.test.tsx`, fixed the same way (hardened to `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(7)` plus explicit slug presence/absence checks). 38 new tests (15 real-Supabase RLS/RPC integration — the sprint's actual DoD proof — plus unit/component tests); `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean; a full `npm run test` run: 281/282 passing, the one failure (`RegisterForm.test.tsx`, a 5000ms timeout) confirmed passing in isolation, matching the pre-existing full-suite resource-contention pattern documented since Sprint 4. Three deliberate scope decisions made and documented, not guessed at: no `agency-logos` Storage bucket/upload UI (a plain `logoUrl` text field instead — the DoD says "create," not "upload a logo"); no new `RH003` custom errcode (the RPC's "reason required" case already fits the existing `23514`→`VALIDATION_ERROR` mapping; a new `P0002`→`PROPERTY_NOT_FOUND` mapping was added instead, for the RPC's actual not-found case); `/admin/properties`/`/admin/bookings` stay `PlaceholderPage` stubs (no DoD dependency on either). Updated `database.md` (§9/§10/§11), `api-design.md` (§3.1/§9/§13/§15.3), and `decisions.md` (ADR-030) in the same change as the code each documents. **Did not commit, push, push the migration, or deploy** — per this project's established pattern, those are separate, later, explicitly-requested steps. The manual real-browser walkthrough (using the newly-seeded `moderator1.seed@rentalhunt.test`/`admin1.seed@rentalhunt.test`) is also still outstanding.

**2026-08-03 — Two real bugs found during manual pre-Sprint-9 testing, fixed, tested, committed, and pushed.** Asked to pause Sprint 9 planning and go back to testing; the developer bootstrapped real admin/agent/moderator accounts directly on the remote production Supabase project (via hand-crafted SQL, since the app has no self-service role-assignment path and `prevent_self_role_change_trigger` blocks self-promotion) and reported: "all logins despite role, all render the homepage on login. They do not redirect to the required pages based on role." Confirmed and fixed in `LoginForm.tsx` — the success handler's fallback redirect was a hardcoded `PATHS.public.home` regardless of the logged-in user's role; added a `roleLandingPath(role)` helper (moderator/admin → `/admin`, customer/agent → `/dashboard`) and switched the mutation's `onSuccess` to read `data.user.role`. Found a related, not-yet-reported latent bug in the same pass: `DashboardPage` branched agent-vs-customer only, so a moderator/admin landing on `/dashboard` directly (bookmark, back button, or this exact redirect bug before the fix) would have silently fallen through to the broken customer viewing-requests view — added an explicit `Navigate` to `/admin` for both roles. Confirmed via `paths.constants.ts`'s own documented route nesting that agents already have their own dashboard content (`AgentDashboardOverviewPage`, rendered at the shared `/dashboard` URL specifically to avoid colliding with the customer's own top-level `/bookings` — a deliberate Sprint 6 architecture choice, not a gap). The developer then asked, in the same session, for a password-visibility toggle: found 7 password fields with no show/hide control across all 4 password-containing forms (Login, Register, Reset Password, Update Password) — past this project's own "extract on 3rd duplicate" threshold, so built one shared, keyboard-operable `PasswordInput` (`shared/ui`, real `aria-label`/`aria-pressed`, no `tabIndex={-1}` shortcut that would have made the toggle keyboard-unreachable) and applied it everywhere rather than patching each form individually. Fixed the resulting test breakage: `LoginForm.test.tsx`'s customer-login assertion updated for the new `/dashboard` redirect target; 6 existing bare `screen.getByLabelText(/password/i)` queries (2 in `LoginForm.test.tsx`, 4 in `RegisterForm.test.tsx`) needed `{ selector: 'input' }` to disambiguate from the new toggle button's own label, rather than weakening the label itself; added a new test asserting the toggle actually reveals/hides the typed value. Verified: `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean; all 5 directly-affected test files pass (2 failures seen once under full-suite parallel load both confirmed passing in isolation); `primaryScreens.a11y.test.tsx` still zero-violation; a full, clean-reset `npm run test` run came back 304/307, with the 3 failures being `PropertiesPage` pagination / Storage RLS upload / `PropertyAnalyticsChart` lazy-load — the same three individually pre-documented full-suite contention flakes from Sprints 4/6/7, confirmed unrelated to anything touched this pass. Committed as two atomic commits, `c90dd55` (redirect fix) and `7b0892c` (password toggle), and pushed to `origin/main`. No `docs/*.md` update needed — a bug fix and a small, self-contained UI addition, neither an architecture/database/API-contract change per `CLAUDE.md` §8. Sprint 9 planning (the three real blockers surfaced earlier — new Supabase production project/tier, real launch reference data, error monitoring — none yet resolved) remains explicitly paused, not abandoned.

**2026-08-04 — Per-role dashboard restructuring: four independent route groups replace the shared `/dashboard`/`/admin` shells; two more real redirect bugs found and fixed.** The developer reported the previous day's redirect fix wasn't working in practice ("the logn page does not redirect after authentication... I have to manually redirect") and that dashboard screens rendered two navbars, then asked for a specific restructuring: remove the shared `/admin` shell and give every role its own dashboard endpoint (`admin-dashboard`/`agent-dashboard`/`moderator-dashboard`/`user-dashboard`). Investigated both reports before proposing anything: the double-navbar was structural (every dashboard route nested inside `AppLayout`, which always renders the public `Header`, stacked underneath the dashboard's own header — present since Sprint 6/7, never flagged); the redirect failure was a genuine, previously-undiscovered cache-race in `useLogin` (`invalidateQueries` is fire-and-forget, so `ProtectedRoute`'s very next render still saw the pre-login cached profile and bounced back to `/login` — invisible against local Supabase's near-zero latency, reliably reproducible against real network latency, which is why it only surfaced once the developer started testing against the real remote project). Presented a plan (`AskUserQuestion` for three genuine product decisions: legacy-URL handling, whether customer gets a full sidebar shell, where `/profile` lives) and got explicit approval before building.

Implemented: `paths.constants.ts` restructured into `adminDashboard`/`moderatorDashboard`/`agentDashboard`/`userDashboard` groups (`authenticated.profile` stays the one shared route); one new generic `DashboardShell` (`widgets/dashboard-shell/`) — sidebar nav + sticky header + account menu — reused by four thin per-role layout widgets instead of four near-duplicated files (crossed the "extract on 3rd duplicate" threshold immediately, since building the 2 newly-needed shells — moderator, customer — as copies of the 2 existing ones would have made four); `routes.tsx` restructured so all four dashboard route groups are **siblings** of `AppLayout`, not children — the actual fix for the double-navbar, since the public `Header`/`Footer` never mount alongside a dashboard shell now; `useLogin.ts` fixed to `setQueryData` the resolved profile synchronously instead of relying on an async refetch to beat `navigate()`; `AgentDashboardLayout` simplified (lost the `children`-vs-`Outlet` duality Sprint 6's Gap 3 needed, since `/agent-dashboard` no longer doubles as another role's landing page); `AdminDashboardLayout` simplified (lost its role-filtered nav-link logic, since moderator no longer shares admin's route tree); `AgentDashboardOverviewPage`/`AdminOverviewPage` un-self-wrapped; new `ModeratorDashboardOverviewPage`/`UserDashboardOverviewPage` extracted from the old role-branching pages; old `DashboardPage.tsx` retired entirely. Moderator reuses admin's exact `AdminVerificationQueuePage`/`AdminActivityLogPage` components (RLS already scopes access — no new data layer, just a new URL/nav). Customer got a real sidebar shell for the first time, with Favorites/Bookings moved under it as nav items rather than unlinked top-level routes. Old paths removed outright, no redirects, per explicit developer decision (pre-launch app, developer-controlled test accounts).

**A third real bug was found during this task's own manual browser verification pass, not inherited from before:** `LoginForm` honored `location.state.from` unconditionally — logging out of an agent account from `/agent-dashboard` (setting `state.from`) and then logging into a *different role's* account sent that role to the agent-only URL, which `ProtectedRoute` bounced to the guest homepage. Reproduced live in the browser (moderator landed on `/` instead of `/moderator-dashboard`) before being traced to this exact mechanism — confirmed the account's `role` was correctly `'moderator'` in the database first, ruling out a seed-data problem, before concluding it was a code bug. Fixed with `isReachableByRole()`: `state.from` is only honored if the logged-in role's own dashboard prefix (or shared `/profile`) actually contains it, otherwise falls back to the role's own landing path. Added a regression test reproducing the exact cross-role scenario, plus a second new test covering a direct admin login (the original reported bug). All 4 roles then re-verified end-to-end via `claude-in-chrome` against a real local dev server, including the specific cross-role scenario that exposed bug #3: correct redirect, exactly one navbar, correct role-scoped nav links, correct sub-page rendering (`/user-dashboard/favorites` checked directly). `claude-in-chrome`'s screenshot/viewport-reporting bug (logged since Sprint 4) and a separate ref-staleness issue (clicking a `find`-returned ref sometimes silently no-ops after an intervening `form_input` call — worked around by clicking at literal screenshot coordinates instead) both recurred but didn't block verification.

One incidental, pre-existing test-fragility fix found and closed while re-verifying `AgentDashboardOverviewPage.test.tsx`: an untimed `findAllByText('0')` (RTL's 1000ms default) could lose a race against a real Supabase round-trip regardless of anything in this task — confirmed via a byte-for-byte diff against the file's only actual change (a cosmetic router-path string) — given an explicit 10s timeout matching its own sibling assertion's already-established convention in the same file. `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean; full suite 305/310, the 5 remaining failures being the same pre-existing, individually-documented full-suite contention class (`PropertiesPage` pagination, `PropertyAnalyticsChart` lazy-load, `LoginForm`, `RegisterForm`, `UpdateEmailForm`) — `LoginForm.test.tsx` itself re-confirmed passing 100% in isolation twice, including both new regression tests. `architecture.md` §6 rewritten (four independent route groups replace the old role-branching table) and a new `decisions.md` **ADR-031** added (recording the reversal of Sprint 7's shared-`/admin`-shell decision and both redirect bugs) in the same change as the code. Committed as 4 atomic commits (`24cfa3c`..`de9289d`) and pushed to `origin/main` at the developer's request.

**2026-08-04 — "View site" link added to every dashboard sidebar.** The developer pointed out the new self-contained dashboard shells (the restructuring above) don't render `AppLayout`'s `Header` at all anymore, so there was no way back to the public site from a dashboard screen — a real, if small, gap the restructuring itself introduced. Added once to the shared `DashboardShell`/new `SidebarContent` wrapper (not duplicated across the four per-role nav-link configs), appearing identically in all four dashboards and the mobile drawer. Verified via `claude-in-chrome`: present with the correct `href="/"` in the customer dashboard's sidebar; since it's the same shared component instance with no per-role branching, its presence there guarantees the other three. `npm run lint`/`typecheck` clean. Committed as `4ed2655` and pushed.

**2026-08-04 — Agent property-form usability pass: a real CSS bug, all 47 counties, searchable/creatable County and Location fields, and a Leaflet+Nominatim map picker.** The developer reported the "New Listing" form's dropdowns visually "collide with the rest of the app info in the background," asked for all 47 Kenya counties (`database.md` had already anticipated this, just deferred), typeahead on the dropdowns, and a map-assisted way to set latitude/longitude (keeping the existing manual-entry fields as a fallback). Investigated each before building: the dropdown bug traced to `--color-popover`/`--color-popover-foreground` never being defined in `styles/index.css` — `Select`/`DropdownMenu` both use `bg-popover`, which is simply invalid in Tailwind v4 when undefined, leaving every dropdown's background fully transparent (page content showed straight through) — fixed with the same values `--color-card` already uses. Counties: added the remaining 43 in a new idempotent migration (the original 4 live in an already-applied, immutable migration, so couldn't be edited directly). Location: `database.md` explicitly documents locations as a lookup table specifically to avoid free-text duplication on `properties` — rather than reverse that, built a shared `Combobox` (`shared/ui/combobox.tsx`, Popover + `cmdk`) with a "create new" affordance that inserts a real `locations` row (new `locations_insert_agent` RLS policy, insert-only — update/delete stay admin-only) when a typed neighborhood has no match. Used `AskUserQuestion` on the one real open decision — which geocoding provider for the map's search box — before building anything; developer chose Nominatim (free, no API key) over a paid Google/Mapbox key or skipping search. Built `LocationPickerMap`/`LocationPickerMapCanvas` mirroring the existing read-only `PropertyMap`/`PropertyMapCanvas` lazy-Leaflet split exactly: click/drag a marker or search a place (up to 5 candidates shown, never auto-picked) writes into the same `latitude`/`longitude` fields the manual inputs already control. Found and fixed one latent, pre-existing correctness bug while touching this code: changing County after picking a Location left the stale, now-wrong `locationId` silently in the form. Verified: a new real-Supabase RLS integration test (agent can insert a location, customer/guest can't, agent's grant doesn't extend to update/delete — proven via `.select()`'s returned rows, since RLS silently zero-matches an update rather than erroring); 4 new `PropertyForm.test.tsx` cases; one new jsdom polyfill needed (`scrollIntoView`, `cmdk` calls it internally). `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean (confirmed via build output that the new Leaflet/cmdk/Popover code stays lazy-loaded — the eager bundle didn't grow); full suite 314/316, the 2 failures matching the exact pre-existing flaky pair already documented repeatedly in this project's history, neither file touched by this work. Manually verified live via `claude-in-chrome`: opaque dropdown background, all 47 counties present and searchable, a real "Runda" location created end-to-end (confirmed via direct DB query, then cleaned up), map click updating the coordinate fields live. `database.md`, `api-design.md` (a `ReferenceDataRepository` contract block that had never been documented there at all), and `ui-guidelines.md` all updated in the same change. **Not committed, pushed, or deployed.** A same-session, separate request ("build admin crud for users") is queued as its own follow-up, not yet started — flagged to the developer that Create/Delete of a real account needs Supabase's Admin API (`service_role`), which this project never exposes to the frontend, likely requiring a new Edge Function.

**2026-08-04 — Admin User CRUD: Invite (Edge Function), Delete (Edge Function), and Edit (Repository) added to `/admin-dashboard/users`.** Picked up as its own task once the property-form work above was done, then explicitly planned (`AskUserQuestion`: Create = email invite over a direct-password-set form; Delete = hard delete only when the account is genuinely empty, over a soft-deactivate-only or cascade-delete option) before writing any code, per `CLAUDE.md` §5. Create/Delete of a real `auth.users` row can't go through RLS/PostgREST — the Admin API needs `service_role`, which this project has never exposed to the frontend (`api-design.md` §12's own decision test: "needs a secret → Edge Function") — so this is the first Edge Function in the project's history, not a Repository-layer addition. Built `supabase/functions/_shared/{cors,adminAuth}.ts` (`requireAdmin(req)`: validates the caller's JWT via an anon-key client, confirms `profiles.role = 'admin'`, returns a `service_role` client scoped to that request — every admin-only Edge Function will reuse this rather than re-deriving it), `admin-invite-user` (`auth.admin.inviteUserByEmail`, sets a non-customer role via `profiles.update` if requested — relying on `prevent_self_role_change_trigger`'s existing `service_role`/admin-acting-on-another-row exemption — logs `activity_logs` `user.invited`), and `admin-delete-user` (checks for blocking activity across properties/bookings/reviews/verification-history before calling `auth.admin.deleteUser`, else a 409 with a per-category breakdown; logs `user.deleted`). Edit (full name/phone) turned out not to need a new Edge Function at all — those are plain `profiles` columns admin already has full RLS write access to (`profiles_update_all_admin`) — extended the existing `AdminUpdateUserSchema`/`admin-user.repository.ts`/`admin-user.service.ts` path instead, the same Hook→Service→Repository chain already used for role/active-status Update. Frontend: `invokeAdminFunction()` helper in the Repository parsing an Edge Function's error `Response` body into the shared `AppError` shape (kept separate from `mapSupabaseError`'s type-guard dispatch, since routing an Edge Function's JSON error body through guards built for PostgREST/GoTrue error shapes risked misclassification); `AdminInviteUserDialog`/`DeleteUserDialog`/`EditUserDetailsDialog` (new); `useInviteUser`/`useDeleteUser`/`useAdminUpdateUser`-reuse hooks; a new `USER_HAS_ACTIVITY` error code end to end. **Two real bugs found via actually running the code, not by inspection, both fixed and re-verified before this was called done:** (1) the first live `admin-delete-user` curl test, against a real seeded agent with zero properties/bookings/reviews but 7 `properties.verified_by` rows, returned a raw 500 (`AuthRetryableFetchError`) instead of a clean 409 — traced via the `functions serve` log to `enforce_verification_authority()`'s trigger blocking even a cascaded `SET NULL` write to `verified_by` when it happens outside the RPC path the trigger actually sanctions, a case the original 4-way blocker check never queried; fixed by adding a 5th `verifiedProperties` check, re-verified via curl (clean 409, correct breakdown, profile survives). (2) `EditUserDetailsDialog.tsx` initially used `AdminUpdateUserSchema.pick({fullName:true, phone:true})` as its Zod resolver schema — Zod throws **at runtime** ("`.pick()` cannot be used on object schemas containing refinements"), invisible to `npm run typecheck`, only surfacing the moment the dialog actually rendered in a real browser (caught via `RouteErrorBoundary`'s fallback during manual verification, not by any prior test — nothing had rendered this component before); fixed with a new standalone `EditUserDetailsSchema` (not derived via `.pick()` from the refined one) and a new `EditUserDetailsDialog.test.tsx` that actually mounts the component, so this exact bug class fails a test automatically in the future. Verified: Deno test files for both functions' pure exported validators exist (`validateInvite`, `validateDeleteRequest`, `hasBlockingActivity` — not run in-session, no standalone `deno` binary on this machine; real behavior instead verified live via `supabase functions serve`'s own bundled Deno runtime + `curl` + Mailpit for the actual invite email); `npx vitest run src/features/admin-users/` → 5 files, 21 tests, all passing; a full live-browser pass via `claude-in-chrome` as `admin1.seed@rentalhunt.test` confirmed Invite (new row appears after a real email round-trip), Delete (409-blocked-with-breakdown on an active account, clean removal on an empty one), and Edit (dialog opens pre-filled, submits, success toast) all work end to end. Full-repo validation re-run clean after both fixes: `npm run lint` (0 errors, only pre-existing warnings), `npm run typecheck` (clean), `npx vitest run` (330/330 in a clean isolated re-run of the two flaky files; a full concurrent run shows 328/330 with those exact same two pre-existing, already-documented full-suite-contention flakes — `PropertiesPage` pagination, `LoginForm` — confirmed unrelated to this work), `npm run build` (clean). `api-design.md` (§9 Manage Users row extended, §12 Edge Functions table gains both new rows including the `verifiedProperties` finding), `database.md` (Policy Summary `locations` row, Tracked Events `user.invited`/`user.deleted` rows), and `decisions.md` (new **ADR-032**, full Decision/Context/Rationale/Consequences including a dedicated paragraph on the `verifiedProperties` discovery) all updated in the same change. Local Supabase stack reset clean (`supabase db reset`) and the dev server stopped at the end of the session. **Not committed, pushed, or deployed.**

**2026-08-04 — Both 2026-08-04 pieces of work committed, pushed, and their infrastructure deployed to remote, at the developer's explicit request.** Split into 7 atomic commits (`3b241f1`..`e7bda28`) mirroring this project's established per-layer commit pattern: CSS-fix+migration → Combobox primitive → `PropertyForm`/`LocationPickerMap` → Edge Functions → `admin-users` frontend → governing docs → `project-state.md`. Two pre-existing, unrelated untracked debug scripts (`frontend/analyze-chunk.cjs`, `frontend/check-contrast.cjs`, leftover from Sprint 8's bundle/contrast investigation) were deliberately left uncommitted — out of scope for this work. Pushed to `origin/main`. Then, on a follow-up explicit request, pushed the pending migration and deployed both Edge Functions to the linked remote project (ref `oqcaythakxgcpgfsqifu`): `supabase migration list` first showed `20260731090000_administration.sql` (Sprint 7) was actually **already** live on remote — this file had it flagged as still-pending, a stale note now corrected — with only `20260804100000_counties_and_agent_location_creation.sql` genuinely missing; `supabase db push` applied it, verified not just via the CLI's exit (a non-fatal migrations-catalog-cache warning is expected and unrelated to the push, per established precedent) but via a direct remote query, `select count(*) from counties` returning **47**. `supabase functions deploy admin-invite-user`/`admin-delete-user` then deployed both, confirmed `ACTIVE` via `supabase functions list`, and a direct unauthenticated `curl` against the live `FUNCTIONS_URL` correctly returned `401` — the auth boundary works in production, not just locally. Neither function has yet been exercised against remote with a real authenticated admin request (only local `curl`/Mailpit verification, per the entry above) — a real smoke test is worth doing once a real admin account exists in production.

**2026-08-04 — Verification Review Page: the moderator/admin decision now happens after seeing the full listing, not a title-only dialog.** The developer's report: "the point of verification is to verify whatever the agent has entered is correct and accurate information" — the Sprint 7 `VerificationActionDialog` never showed anything to actually verify against. Presented two design options via `AskUserQuestion` before building (a bigger inline panel vs. a dedicated page reusing the public listing-detail layout); developer picked the dedicated page. No schema/RLS change needed: `propertyRepository.getByIdAdmin(id)` already existed (previously only consumed internally by `verificationRepository.history()`) and RLS's `properties_select_all_moderator_admin` policy was already the authority — only a thin `adminVerificationService.getById()` passthrough and a `useVerificationProperty` hook were added. New `AdminVerificationReviewPage` (routed at `/admin-dashboard/verification-queue/:id` and the moderator-dashboard equivalent, mirroring the queue page's own existing dual-mount pattern) reuses `PropertyGallery`/`PriceBreakdown`/`PropertyMap`/`AmenitiesGrid`/`AgentCard`/`VerificationBadge` in the exact same order the public `PropertyDetailPage` already uses. `VerificationActionDialog` (and its test) deleted outright, replaced by `VerificationActionBar` — reason field + Reject/Approve fixed to the bottom of the page, sticky on mobile / in-flow on `sm:`+, the same breakpoint pattern `ViewingCTA` already established. `VerificationQueueTable`'s "Review" button now navigates instead of opening a dialog. **A real gap was found in the new test itself, not the app, via a live manual browser pass:** `AdminVerificationReviewPage.test.tsx`'s fixtures (`pending_verification` properties) had no `afterAll` cleanup, and a manual click-through afterward found the real Verification Queue cluttered with stray "Review Test..." rows left behind by prior test runs — fixed by adding tracked-id cleanup, the same pattern `verification.rls.test.ts` already established for exactly this reason. **A second real, unrelated environment issue recurred during this session's validation, matching a documented Sprint 8 finding exactly:** after `supabase db reset`, the `vector` container entered a restart loop; confirmed the DB/Auth/REST layer itself was actually healthy throughout (a subset of tests passed fine) and `vector` is purely Supabase's log-shipping sidecar, not something the app or its tests depend on — a full `supabase stop` + `start` cleared it. Verified: 3 new real-Supabase integration tests (full-listing render, Approve genuinely updating `properties.verification_status` in the database, Reject blocked-then-persisted-with-reason, the last two checked via a direct follow-up `serviceClient` query, not just UI state) plus 3 `VerificationActionBar` component tests (net 332 total, up from 330, after deleting the dialog's 4). Manually verified live via `claude-in-chrome` as `admin1.seed@rentalhunt.test`: reviewed a real seeded pending listing end to end (every field rendered correctly), rejected it with no reason (blocked inline), rejected it with a reason (confirmed via direct DB query afterward), reviewed and approved a second fixture (confirmed removed from the queue). `npm run lint` (0 errors)/`typecheck` (clean)/`build` (clean — `AdminVerificationReviewPage` its own lazy chunk)/`npx vitest run` (331/332 in a full run, the one failure the same pre-existing `LoginForm` contention flake, confirmed passing in isolation). `ui-guidelines.md` (new §12.14.1) and `api-design.md` (§9's Verification Queue/Action rows extended, new Verification Review row) updated in the same change — no `database.md` change needed. Local Supabase stack reset clean and dev server stopped at the end of the session. **Not committed, pushed, or deployed.**

**2026-08-04 — Session-management hardening: password policy, sign-out-of-other-devices, admin/moderator idle timeout, dev-only per-tab sessions (ADR-033).** After committing/pushing the Verification Review Page, the developer asked to "talk about session management in this app" — answered by walking through `AuthProvider`'s single-cache design, `ProtectedRoute`'s UX-only role (RLS is the real boundary), and this project's own two real historical bugs in this area (the `useLogin` cache-race and `LoginForm`'s `state.from` reachability bug, both already documented). The developer then reported a genuine pain point from their own testing — unable to have admin/agent/customer all signed in in the same browser at once — and asked for best-practice recommendations given Supabase Auth specifically, then to sequence and build them. Sequenced foundational-first: (1) an Auth-config audit, (2) sign-out-everywhere + idle timeout, (3) the dev multi-actor tool.

**(1) Auth-config audit** found one real, concrete, previously-undiscovered gap: `supabase/config.toml`'s server-side `minimum_password_length`/`password_requirements` were still Supabase's own weak defaults (`6`, no complexity) despite `passwordSchema`/`api-design.md` §14 already documenting an 8-char+letter+digit policy since Sprint 2 — meaning a direct API call bypassing the frontend could set a 6-character password with no requirements at all. Fixed in `config.toml` (`8`/`"letters_digits"` — not the stricter `lower_upper_...` variant, which would reject passwords the frontend itself already accepts); confirmed live via a raw `curl` signup with a weak password now genuinely returning `weak_password`. Also produced a checklist of settings to verify/set on the production Supabase Dashboard (which this session has no credentials/API access to change directly) — `secure_password_change`, refresh-token rotation, rate limits — folded into the existing local/remote Auth-config-parity Technical Debt row rather than a new one.

**(2) "Sign out of all other devices"** (`features/profile-management`): two design decisions made explicitly during implementation, not assumed — `credentialsRepository.signOutOtherDevices()` uses `supabase.auth.signOut({ scope: 'others' })`, not the `'global'` scope originally suggested in the session-management discussion, since `'others'` matches Google/GitHub/Slack's own "sign out everywhere else" shape (revokes every *other* session, leaves the one you're using signed in) rather than also logging the caller out of the page they clicked the button from. New `SignOutOtherDevicesSection` on `ProfilePage`, with a confirm `Dialog` (mirrors `DeleteUserDialog`'s pattern) since it's a real, immediate, unretractable action. **Verified with a genuine second Supabase session, not a mock:** the test signs in as the same user from a second independent client (`rlsTestHelpers.ts`'s `actorClient()`), clicks through the UI in the rendered component (using the app's real shared `supabase` client), then confirms the second client's `refreshSession()` now fails (its token was actually revoked) while the rendered component's own session (`supabase.auth.getUser()`) still succeeds.

**(3) A 15-minute idle timeout for `admin`/`moderator` only**, with a 60-second "Stay signed in" warning first — both the exact duration and the warn-vs-silent behavior were put to the developer via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed, since neither has an objectively correct answer. New generic `useIdleTimer` hook (no auth knowledge, tracks mousemove/keydown/click/scroll/touchstart) + `IdleSessionGuard` component (role-gated, reuses the existing `useLogout` mutation — no separate sign-out path), mounted once in `App.tsx` so it covers an admin/moderator anywhere they're signed in, not just `/admin-dashboard/*`. **A real React Compiler lint error was hit and fixed while building this:** the first version called `reset()` (which sets state) directly inside the mount `useEffect`, triggering a new "`setState` synchronously within an effect" rule — fixed by splitting `armTimers()` (schedules timers, no `setState` of its own, safe to call from the mount effect) from `reset()` (also clears visible state, only ever invoked from a genuine callback — an activity-event listener or the "Stay signed in" button — never synchronously in an effect body). Tested with `vi.useFakeTimers()` (a pattern new to this codebase, established here): 4 hook-level tests, 4 component-level tests (role-gating, the warning appearing/dismissing, sign-out firing on schedule).

**(4) Dev-only per-tab session isolation** — the piece that actually addresses the developer's stated pain point, and a smaller, more elegant solution than the originally-planned bespoke "multi-actor testing tool": `shared/lib/supabase.ts` now uses `sessionStorage` instead of `localStorage` for the Supabase client's session storage, but only when `env.isDev` (new field on `env`, keeping `env.ts` the sole `import.meta.env` read site). Since `sessionStorage` is scoped per-browser-tab (unlike `localStorage`, shared across every tab on the same origin), every dev-server tab now gets an independent real session automatically — no custom UI, no separate client instances, just the browser's own existing isolation mechanism. Never applied in production (`env.isDev` is statically `false` in a production build, so the branch is dead code) — a real user opening a link in a new tab still expects their session to carry over, which this would break. **Manually verified, not just unit-tested:** opened two fresh tabs against the same running dev server, signed into one as `agent1.seed@rentalhunt.test` and the other as `customer1.seed@rentalhunt.test`, and confirmed both simultaneously and independently authenticated (`/agent-dashboard` and `/user-dashboard` respectively) on the same origin at the same time — the exact scenario the developer said they couldn't do before. A third tab (attempting `admin1.seed@rentalhunt.test`) hit repeated `claude-in-chrome` click-registration flakiness (confirmed via network-request tracking that no auth call was even sent — a tool interaction issue, not an app bug, using credentials that worked reliably everywhere else this session) — accepted the two-tab proof as sufficient given the mechanism itself was already conclusively demonstrated.

Verified overall: `npm run lint` (0 errors), `npm run typecheck` (clean), `npm run build` (clean), `npx vitest run` — a genuinely clean 345/345 full run, not even the usual pre-existing flaky test failing this time. `api-design.md` (new §5.11) and `ui-guidelines.md` (new §11.21) updated in the same change; `decisions.md` gained **ADR-033** covering all four pieces and their rationale. No `database.md` change (no schema/RLS touched). Local Supabase stack reset clean at the end of the session; dev server stopped. **Not committed, pushed, or deployed.**

**2026-08-04 — Customer Dashboard Welcome Block: fixed the "skeleton page" a brand-new customer sees after login.** The developer, after committing/pushing the session-management work, asked to talk about the landing page — specifically disliking "the skeleton page that we get after login" — and asked for a plan for a landing page with site info, how-to-use guidance, and a link to Browse Properties, plus an inventory of what other pages the site should have or is missing. Diagnosed before proposing anything: the page in question is `UserDashboardOverviewPage` (`/user-dashboard`), a real component, not a stub — just genuinely bare for any brand-new customer (two booking sections, both legitimately empty). Distinct from `HomePage`, which already has a real pre-login landing page. Forked a research pass across `roadmap.md`/`user-stories.md`/`requirements.md`/`branding.md`/`decisions.md` before presenting a plan, so the recommendation was grounded in what this project has already decided rather than invented: confirmed `branding.md`'s Tagline and Brand Promise are real, reusable copy; confirmed `Footer.tsx`'s own comment has flagged "no footer link/content spec exists yet" since Sprint 1 (About/Contact/Terms genuinely missing, never scoped anywhere); found the catch-all 404 route reuses `PlaceholderPage`, whose text ("Route skeleton — page not yet implemented") is actively misleading for a real not-found case, not just an unbuilt one. Presented the plan plus this page inventory (genuinely missing vs. deliberately deferred `FUT-*` items), then used `AskUserQuestion` on two real product decisions before building: welcome block always visible vs. first-time-only (developer chose **always visible**) and task scope (developer chose **just this page** — About/Contact/Terms/404 explicitly deferred as their own later task, since they need real content decisions this session can't make alone). Built inline in `UserDashboardOverviewPage.tsx` — no new component/feature slice, since it's static single-use content: the tagline as the page's `<h1>`, a 3-step "how it works" row (Search → Compare → Book a Viewing — this wording is the page's own, not quoted from `branding.md`), a `Browse Properties` button, and static trust-indicator chips mirroring `SearchHero`'s own already-established homepage pattern (not a new visual language). Existing bookings sections renested for correct heading hierarchy (`<h1>` → `<h2>` "Your Bookings" → `<h3>` per section). **A real, legitimate pre-existing test breakage was found and fixed, not a regression in the new markup:** `primaryScreens.a11y.test.tsx`'s `UserDashboardOverviewPage` case waited for the literal text "Dashboard" — the page's old `<h1>`, now removed by this change — before running its axe check; fixed to wait for the new tagline heading instead, and the axe check itself then confirmed zero real violations in the new markup. Verified: a new `UserDashboardOverviewPage.test.tsx` (none existed before) proving the welcome block renders with a working `/properties` link for a brand-new customer with zero bookings, alongside still-correctly-empty booking sections; manually verified live via `claude-in-chrome` as `customer1.seed@rentalhunt.test` (real booking history) — welcome block and real bookings render together correctly, "Browse Properties" navigates to `/properties`, zero console errors. `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean; full suite 344/346, both failures the exact pre-existing `PropertiesPage`/`LoginForm` contention flakes, confirmed passing in isolation. `ui-guidelines.md` (new §13.12) updated in the same change — no `api-design.md`/`database.md` change (pure static UI content). Local Supabase stack reset clean and dev server stopped at the end of the session. **Not committed, pushed, or deployed.**

**2026-08-05 — Missing-pages survey properly delivered, after discovering a prior session had gone out of process attempting the same ask.** The developer opened this session by asking for project status; that surfaced three untracked files `git status` still showed from an unfinished prior session: `supabase/migrations/20260804110000_contact_messages.sql` and two debug scripts. Investigated before touching anything: the migration was a real, competently-written `contact_messages` table + RLS policy set, but its header comment cited `database.md §5.16`, `api-design.md §5.12`, and "ADR-034" — none of which existed in the repo, and no frontend code referenced the table anywhere. The developer confirmed the context: a prior session had been asked to "find out what missing pages we didn't have and plan for their implementation," and had instead jumped straight to an unapproved, unfinished schema change without ever delivering the actual survey/plan — the same class of process failure as the Sprint 8 background-agent incident, skipping `CLAUDE.md` §6/§8's Requirements → Architecture → Database ordering and "docs before implementation" rule. **Developer decision: discard the orphaned migration** rather than adopt/repair it (deleted this session); the two leftover debug scripts (`analyze-chunk.cjs`/`check-contrast.cjs`, matched to Sprint 8's bundle/contrast work) were left in place, still undecided — see Technical Debt.

The actual survey was then done properly: checked `routes/routes.tsx`/`paths.constants.ts` and every nav component (`Footer.tsx`/`Header.tsx`/`MobileNavDrawer.tsx`) directly for dead links (none — `Footer.tsx` is deliberately empty, flagged since Sprint 1) and cross-checked `requirements.md`/`user-stories.md`/`roadmap.md` for existing scope (none). Confirmed real, never-scoped gaps: About/How-It-Works, Contact (+ admin review), Terms of Service, Privacy Policy, and the catch-all `*` route's misleading `PlaceholderPage` copy standing in for a genuine 404. Presented the survey and a draft plan, then resolved two real product decisions via `AskUserQuestion` before writing anything: Contact delivery — **admin-review-only, no outbound email** (same call the deleted migration had already made, avoids new email-provider infra); roadmap placement — **folded into Sprint 9 (Production Launch) as pre-launch prep scope**, not a new dedicated sprint. Per the developer's explicit "plan first" scope, only documentation was written this session, no code or schema: `requirements.md` new §5.4 "Static & Legal Content" (`FR-CONTENT-001`–`005`); `user-stories.md` new Epic 11 "Static & Legal Content" (`CONTENT-001`–`005`, full User Story + Acceptance Criteria, plus MVP Scope Summary and Story-to-Epic Traceability Table rows); `roadmap.md` §13 (Sprint 9) gained a new Scope bullet and a Release Checklist line gating `v1.0.0` on real (not placeholder) Terms/Privacy legal text, which only the developer can supply. `database.md`/`api-design.md`/`architecture.md` deliberately left untouched — those get written when `CONTENT-002`/`003` (the only story pair needing schema) actually enters implementation, not speculatively ahead of it. No dev server or local Supabase stack was started this session (pure documentation work).

**2026-08-05 — Same session, developer said "build": `CONTENT-001`/`002`/`003`/`005` implemented (Terms/Privacy deliberately skipped, still blocked on real legal text).** Followed `CLAUDE.md` §6's layer order throughout: migration (`20260805090000_contact_messages.sql`, `contact_messages` table + RLS) → `database.md` §5.16/§9 → `api-design.md` new §23 Contact API (old §23 Claude Code Implementation Rules renumbered to §24, with `coding-standards.md`'s two cross-references to it fixed in the same change) → `entities/contact-message` → `features/contact`/`features/admin-messages` → four new pages → routing → `ui-guidelines.md` → tests. New `decisions.md` **ADR-034** (admin-review-only Contact form, no outbound email — resolved via `AskUserQuestion`, matching the deleted migration's own original instinct). `Footer.tsx` gets its first-ever real links (About/Contact; Terms/Privacy deliberately withheld until they exist). `AboutPage` reuses `UserDashboardOverviewPage`'s welcome-block content verbatim rather than re-inventing it, since a guest never sees that page. `NotFoundPage` is the first real implementation of `ui-guidelines.md` §19's already-documented "404" `EmptyState` row.

**Two real, non-obvious RLS bugs found via failing integration tests, not assumed from the policy table — both traced to the same root cause (no role but admin can read `contact_messages` at all, not even a submitter's own row):** (1) `contactMessageRepository.submit()`'s first draft chained `.select().single()` after `.insert()`, this project's usual pattern — failed `42501`, since a guest/customer can never read back what they just wrote here. Fixed: `submit()` returns `void`. (2) The rate-limit check's first draft used a plain `.select('*', { count: 'exact', head: true })` — failed with a bare `401` for `anon`, since even a headless count-only request still needs the table's SELECT grant, which `anon` was never given. Fixed with a new `security definer` RPC, `count_recent_contact_messages_by_email()`, mirroring `current_role()`/`property_ids_with_all_amenities()`'s existing narrow-elevated-privilege pattern. Both found and fixed by actually running the real integration tests against a freshly reset local stack, not by reasoning about the policy table in the abstract — full detail and two new general rules recorded in `database.md` §9. Local Supabase `db reset` hit the same `storage`/`vector`-container-unhealthy issue already logged in Sprint 8/session-management-hardening; resolved the same documented way (`stop` + `start` + `db reset`), not investigated as a new issue.

Verified: 19 new tests, all passing — `contact-message.repository.test.ts` (unit, fake client), `contact-message.rls.test.ts` (4 real-Supabase RLS integration tests, including both bugs above), `contact.service.test.ts` (rate-limit + Zod validation), `ContactForm.test.tsx` (real-Supabase integration: guest submit, short-message validation error, signed-in name/email prefill). Full suite 364/365 — the one failure (`PropertyAnalyticsChart`'s lazy-load test) confirmed passing in isolation, the same pre-existing full-suite-contention flakiness class documented since Sprint 6, unrelated to this work. `npm run lint` (0 errors), `tsc -b` (clean), `npm run build` (clean — every new page its own lazy chunk, e.g. `AboutPage` 2.36 kB / `ContactPage` 4.13 kB / `AdminMessagesPage` 3.71 kB / `NotFoundPage` 0.95 kB gzipped-source sizes, no eager-bundle regression). **Manual real-browser verification could not be completed this session** — `claude-in-chrome` failed to connect at all (`tabs_context_mcp` → "Browser extension is not connected"), tried twice, a different and more severe failure than the viewport/screenshot-reporting bug logged since Sprint 4 — stated explicitly per `CLAUDE.md`'s own instruction rather than assumed or silently skipped (new Technical Debt row). Dev server left running at `localhost:5199` against the (also left running) local Supabase stack for the developer to check directly. **Not committed, pushed, or deployed.**

**2026-08-05 — Same session, developer asked to "check and resolve the debug scripts": `analyze-chunk.cjs`/`check-contrast.cjs` kept as permanent tooling, one real active bug fixed along the way.** Decision: keep both rather than delete, since `analyze-chunk.cjs` is a direct companion to the already-permanent `rollup-plugin-visualizer`/`ANALYZE=1` setup `vite.config.ts` has carried since Sprint 8, and `check-contrast.cjs` checks a permanent product requirement (`requirements.md` §13.4 accessibility) that already caught one real bug once. **Before wiring it up, actually ran it — and found it was wrong.** `check-contrast.cjs` hardcoded a snapshot of the design tokens inline rather than reading `styles/index.css`, and that snapshot had silently drifted: it still held `secondary-foreground: #FFFFFF`, the value from *before* Sprint 8's own fix, while the real CSS has held the corrected `#0f172a` ever since. Running the script as found would have reported a false, permanent FAIL on the exact pair it was built to catch. Rewritten to parse `src/styles/index.css`'s `@theme` block directly via regex instead of duplicating a copy — eliminates this entire class of future drift, not just this one stale value. Re-ran and confirmed: `secondary-foreground` now reports `4.74`, matching Sprint 8's documented fix number exactly. (The one other FAIL the script reports, `subtle-foreground` on `background` at `2.45` against a `3.0` threshold, is unchanged from before this fix and was already implicitly accepted by Sprint 8 — not touched, out of scope for a script-correctness pass.) `analyze-chunk.cjs` was verified working against a real `ANALYZE=1 npm run build`, then given a proper usage header (the exact chunk-name format — `assets/`-prefixed, hash-suffixed — was previously undocumented and had to be reverse-engineered from `stats.json` itself), a friendly missing-file message, and an "available chunks" listing on a no-match instead of a bare error. Both wired as `npm run analyze:chunk`/`npm run check:contrast` — on-demand diagnostics, deliberately not CI gates (neither ever was one). `npm run lint` still 0 errors (root-level `.cjs` files aren't linted, unaffected). No `docs/*.md` update — internal dev tooling, not a documented convention section, matching how the visualizer itself was integrated (inline `vite.config.ts` comments only).

**2026-08-05 — Same date, `claude-in-chrome` reconnected: real mobile QA pass across all four roles found and fixed a genuine, high-impact bug.** The developer asked for a `claude-in-chrome` mobile-viewport test across Admin/Agent/Moderator/Customer. `resize_window` was confirmed non-functional again (same as every prior session), and this time even a developer-driven manual window resize didn't help — the extension was controlling a different window than the one visible/resizable to the developer. Fixed by opening a fresh tab (which does land in the developer's real window) and having them resize that specific tab/window; confirmed via `window.innerWidth`, not assumed. Logged in as `customer1`/`agent1`/`moderator1`/`admin1.seed@rentalhunt.test` in turn (via direct JS form-filling, since mouse-click automation threw frequent CDP timeouts all session — some clicks still landed, some didn't, verified after each one rather than trusted). Checked `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` vs `window.innerWidth` on every screen — a numeric ground-truth check, since screenshots were also frequently slow/unreliable to capture.

**Found real horizontal overflow on 3 of ~15 screens checked, across 2 roles:** Customer Dashboard Overview (57px overflow), the brand-new admin Contact Messages screen (215px), and the Verification Review page's property gallery (325px — the worst case). Root-caused via a live DOM patch (`main.style.minWidth = '0px'` in the browser) *before* writing any fix, and it resolved all three instantly and completely: `DashboardShell.tsx`'s `<main>` is a flex item in a row (`aside` + `main`) with the browser's default `min-width: auto`, which refuses to let a flex item shrink below its content's own unbroken min-content width — at `>= lg` this never showed (ample room), but at mobile widths, any page with wide-enough content (a booking-card row, a data table, an image gallery) forced the *entire page* to scroll horizontally, even though each of those pieces already handles overflow correctly in isolation (confirmed on the clean Activity Log page — `shared/ui/table.tsx`'s own `overflow-auto` wrapper works fine; it was the ancestor `<main>` forcing the whole page wide around it). Confirmed the public site is unaffected — `AppLayout`'s `<main>` sits in a column flex, not a row, so the same bug class structurally can't occur there (clean `PropertyDetailPage` result matches).

**Fixed with one class** — `min-w-0` added to `DashboardShell.tsx`'s `<main>`, plus a comment recording the mechanism/numbers for future readers, since a bare utility class addition would read as decorative otherwise. Shared by all four role dashboards, so one fix applies uniformly. **Re-verified live after the fix** (Vite HMR picked it up automatically) — all three previously-broken pages re-checked fresh with new logins: Customer Overview 393px (was 465), admin Contact Messages 393px with its table wrapper correctly shrinking to 361px (was 623, wrapper wasn't shrinking at all), Verification Review 393px (was 733). Also covered the two screens the first pass didn't reach: Admin Analytics (clean) and the Verification Review page's actual Approve button (clicked via JS, then confirmed via a direct `psql` query against the local DB that `properties.verification_status` genuinely flipped to `verified` — not just trusted the UI's own toast). `npm run lint`/`tsc -b`/`npm run build` all clean after the fix.

**Two secondary findings, not fixed this pass:** leftover RLS/adversarial-test rows visible in the *live* Agent Properties list and Moderator Verification Queue (same incident class Sprint 8 already logged once, apparently recurred via this session's own earlier test runs — needs `afterAll` cleanup in the test files, not another one-off reset; new Technical Debt row). And one real, deliberate side effect of testing the Approve button for real: seeded property `a4000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000006` ("Cozy Studio in Kileleshwa") is now `verified` rather than `pending_verification` — left as-is rather than reverted, matching this project's own precedent (e.g. Sprint 7's "Karura Realty" agency) of keeping genuine functional-test side effects rather than a synthetic-feeling revert. Dev server and local Supabase stack left running throughout. **Fix not committed, pushed, or deployed.**

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| **Features Complete** | 47 / 61 user stories committed/pushed/deployed and verified live (`AUTH-001`–`AUTH-006`, `SYS-002` — Epic 1; `DISC-001`–`DISC-006`, `SYS-003` — Epic 2; `PROP-001`–`PROP-006` — Epic 3; `FAV-001`–`003`, `VIEW-001`–`005`, `CUST-001`–`004` — Epics 4/5; `AGENT-001`–`008`, `BOOK-001`–`006` — Epic 6, now live) |

| **Documentation Progress** | 13 / 13 governing documents complete (100%) |
| **Known Bugs** | 0 shipped-and-open. Real bugs/gaps found and fixed, never left unaddressed: the `PropertyDetailPage`/`useProperty` not-found retry bug (found 2026-07-28, fixed and deployed 2026-07-29, `f4218d3`); the Sprint 5 RLS regression and admin-grant gap below (found and fixed before any deploy). (Sprint 2) missing table-level `GRANT`s and `prevent_self_role_change` blocking `service_role`; an unreachable `otp_expired` mapping; no documented email/password-change API contract; production registration silently failing due to a remote Supabase Auth config default. (Sprint 3) a `language sql` function created before the table it queries; `database.md`'s own prose calling `agent_directory` "security invoker" when only `security definer` achieves its stated purpose; a `React.lazy` performance fix tried, re-measured, and found not to help (deliberately parked, not silently declared fixed). (Sprint 4) `api-design.md` §6.2's stale `agents`/`profiles`-join example and unneeded `increment_view_count` RPC mention. (Sprint 5) a blanket guest-permission RLS regression on `properties` (new customer-visibility policies missing `to authenticated`) and a missing admin `DELETE` grant on `viewing_requests` — both found via the RLS test suite and fixed before merge, never deployed; two pre-existing test files that crashed (not flaked) after gaining an unmet `AuthProvider` dependency, also fixed. (Sprint 6) a fail-open `<>`-vs-`is distinct from` bug in `submit_property_for_verification`'s permission guard, found via direct testing against a no-auth-context session; a `File.type` reliability issue in the Storage-upload test environment; a real cross-agency data leak in `listForAgent`/`getSummary`/`listPropertyAnalytics` (RLS's guest-visibility policy OR-combining with the agent-specific one, letting other agencies' guest-visible properties show up in an agent's own management views — confirmed read-only, not a write vulnerability) found via the manual two-agent browser pass and fixed with explicit `agency_id` filters; a pre-existing `seed.sql` bug (missing `confirmation_token`/etc. on seeded `auth.users` rows, breaking login — not signup — for every seeded user, agents and customers alike) found while trying to run that same manual pass and fixed. (Sprint 7) an early draft of `set_property_verification()` inserted a redundant `activity_logs` row that `database.md` §11 had already explicitly decided against — caught rereading that section, not by a test, and removed before anything shipped; a Realtime-hook/jsdom-teardown WebSocket exception and a cross-file guest-count test-pollution issue (both the same known environment-limitation/test-pollution classes Sprint 6 already hit once, fixed the same way) — all found and fixed before any of it shipped. (Sprint 8) an `AgentDashboardOverviewPage` test failure that three sprints of history had dismissed as full-suite flakiness turned out to be a real hardcoded-date test bug (not an app bug), root-caused and fixed; a background research agent's unauthorized production-code/credentials-file edits, individually reviewed and kept per developer decision (see Active Sprint). (Post-Sprint-8, found via manual pre-Sprint-9 testing, 2026-08-03) `LoginForm` redirecting every successful login to the guest homepage regardless of role, and `DashboardPage` having no moderator/admin branch — both fixed (`c90dd55`); no password field in the app (7 fields, 4 forms) offering a show/hide toggle — fixed with a shared `PasswordInput` component (`7b0892c`). (Post-Sprint-8, 2026-08-04, committed `24cfa3c`..`de9289d`/`4ed2655`) a genuine `useLogin` cache-race — `invalidateQueries` being fire-and-forget meant `ProtectedRoute`'s next render could still see the pre-login cached profile and bounce back to `/login`, invisible locally but reliable against real network latency — fixed with a synchronous `setQueryData`; a structural double-navbar on every dashboard screen (public `AppLayout` header rendering underneath the dashboard's own) — fixed by moving all four dashboard route groups out of `AppLayout`'s children; `LoginForm` honoring `state.from` unconditionally, letting a cross-role re-login land on a URL the new role can't reach and get bounced to the guest homepage — found live in the browser during this task's own verification pass, fixed with `isReachableByRole()`. (Post-Sprint-8, 2026-08-04, uncommitted) `--color-popover`/`--color-popover-foreground` never defined in `styles/index.css`, leaving every `Select`/`DropdownMenu` fully transparent — page content showed through, reported as dropdowns "colliding" with the background; fixed with the same values `--color-card` already uses. A latent form bug found while touching the same code, not previously reported: changing County after already picking a Location left the stale, now-invalid `locationId` silently in the form instead of clearing it. (Post-Sprint-8, 2026-08-04, uncommitted, Admin User CRUD) `admin-delete-user`'s blocker check missed `properties.verified_by` entirely — a real seeded agent with zero properties/bookings/reviews but 7 properties they'd verified caused a raw 500 instead of a clean 409, since `enforce_verification_authority()`'s trigger blocks even a cascaded `SET NULL` write outside its sanctioned RPC path — found via the first live curl test, fixed with a 5th blocker check; `EditUserDetailsDialog.tsx` used `AdminUpdateUserSchema.pick(...)` on a schema built with `.refine()`, which Zod throws on at runtime (not caught by `tsc -b`) the moment the component actually renders — found live in the browser via `RouteErrorBoundary`'s fallback, fixed with a new standalone, non-derived schema plus a new test that actually mounts the component so this bug class is caught automatically going forward. (Post-Sprint-8, 2026-08-04, uncommitted, Verification Review Page) `AdminVerificationReviewPage.test.tsx`'s own fixtures (`pending_verification` properties) had no cleanup — a real gap in the test itself, found via a live manual browser pass that turned up stray "Review Test..." rows cluttering the real Verification Queue; fixed by adding `afterAll` cleanup, mirroring `verification.rls.test.ts`'s existing pattern. (Post-Sprint-8, 2026-08-04, uncommitted, Session Management/ADR-033) server-side password policy (`config.toml`) was still Supabase's own weak default (6 chars, no complexity) despite `passwordSchema`/`api-design.md` §14 already documenting 8-char+letter+digit since Sprint 2 — a direct API call bypassing the frontend could set a 6-character password with no requirements at all; fixed and confirmed live via a raw `curl` signup now genuinely rejected. Also a real React Compiler lint error, not a functional bug: `useIdleTimer`'s first version called `setState` synchronously inside its mount `useEffect`, caught by lint before it ever shipped, fixed by moving the state-resetting logic out of the mount path and into genuine callbacks only. (Post-Sprint-8, 2026-08-04, uncommitted, Customer Dashboard Welcome Block) `primaryScreens.a11y.test.tsx`'s `UserDashboardOverviewPage` axe check waited for the literal text "Dashboard," the page's old `<h1>`, legitimately removed by this change — a real, expected test breakage from an intentional content change, not a regression in the new markup; fixed by waiting for the new tagline heading instead, after which the axe check confirmed zero real violations. (Post-Sprint-8, 2026-08-05, uncommitted, Home/About merge + date/time pickers + view-count + agency name) `TimePicker`'s first draft derived its displayed value purely from the external prop with no local state, meaning a user could never actually complete a time selection through the UI at all (picking an hour alone gave zero feedback, and the next pick always saw a stale empty hour) — found via extensive debugging that first ruled out the test environment itself, fixed with a real local-state + render-time-prop-sync pattern; a genuine production-bundle regression found via `analyze-chunk.cjs` during final validation, not by any test — `react-day-picker`/`date-fns` leaked into the eager `index-*.js` chunk (330→402 kB) because five eagerly-loaded auth components had never been migrated to Sprint 8's own direct-import convention and still pulled the growing `@/shared/ui` barrel, fixed by converting all five to direct imports (eager chunk now 280 kB, below the pre-existing baseline). |

Sprint 9 (Production Launch) not yet started — three real blockers (new Supabase production project/tier, real launch reference data, error monitoring) surfaced but unresolved, and planning is explicitly paused pending the developer's decisions. # Session Notes

_Append one entry per session, most recent last. Never edit or delete a prior entry — see Claude Instructions._

- **2026-07-17:** Completed the full documentation baseline — all ten `docs/*.md` files, `CLAUDE.md`, and this `project-state.md`. Sprint 0 closed. Sprint 1 (Project Foundation) is now the active sprint. No application code written yet.
- **2026-07-18:** Added `docs/decisions.md`, an 18-entry ADR log recording the rationale, alternatives, and revisit criteria behind every major decision baked into the existing documentation set. Sprint 1 remains the active sprint and is still not started — this was a documentation-consistency task, not sprint work.
- **2026-07-21:** Implemented FEAT-001 (Workspace Bootstrap), the first application-code task. Notable decisions made along the way (all stated as assumptions per `CLAUDE.md` §5, since docs were silent or scaffold defaults conflicted with approved docs):
  - No `tasks/Sprint-01/FEAT-001-workspace-bootstrap.md` existed when this task began; it was drafted this session (scoped to workspace-bootstrap only, deferring the rest of `roadmap.md` §5's Sprint 1 list to later FEAT tickets) since the task instruction directed deriving it from `roadmap.md`.
  - Vite's current `react-ts` scaffold defaults to `oxlint` instead of ESLint. Replaced it with ESLint (flat config) because `coding-standards.md` §3.3/§6 specifically requires `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any`, `import/no-cycle`, and an FSD boundary rule — none of which oxlint provides in the documented form. This is a case of following already-decided docs over a tool's new default, not an invented deviation.
  - Used `eslint-plugin-import-x` (the maintained fork) instead of `eslint-plugin-import`, which doesn't yet support ESLint 10 (peer dependency conflict). Same `no-cycle` rule, different package name.
  - `eslint-plugin-boundaries` v6/v7's real config API turned out to differ substantially from its own README's "Quick Example" (which documents v7's `policies` key) — v6.0.2 (installed) actually uses a `rules` key with `{ from: { type }, allow: [{ to: { type } }] }` entries, and element classification requires `mode: 'full'` with a recursive (`**`) pattern for layers like `app/` that don't have per-slice subfolders (the default `mode: 'folder'` only works for layers like `entities/*` that do, e.g. `entities/property/`). Also needed `eslint-import-resolver-typescript` wired in as `settings['import/resolver']`, since directory/index imports (e.g. `from '../../app'`) don't resolve without it — without that resolver, the boundaries rule silently no-ops on every barrel import. Verified the final rule actually fires by deliberately creating and lint-checking a throwaway cross-layer violation file before deleting it.
  - Replaced Vite's demo-branded `index.css` (purple accent tokens, fixed-width `#root`, Vite/React branding) with a minimal, neutral CSS reset — the real `ui-guidelines.md` §21 design tokens are out of scope for this task and shouldn't be pre-empted by leftover scaffold branding.
  - Deferred the FSD "no sibling-feature imports" refinement (`coding-standards.md` §3.2) — the coarse cross-layer direction rule is verified working, but the capture-based same-slice rule needs at least two real feature folders to test meaningfully against, and multiple hallucinated/deprecated config shapes were encountered for this plugin during this session; logged as technical debt for Sprint 2 rather than shipped unverified.

  Verified: `npm run dev` serves correctly (resolving through the `app/` barrel), `npm run build` succeeds, `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` both pass clean. Sprint 1 remains active; FEAT-001 is the only Sprint 1 item closed so far.
- **2026-07-21 (review pass):** FEAT-001 reviewed and approved. At the user's request, also reviewed FEAT-003 (Project Structure/FSD) — no task file existed for it either, so it was drafted (`tasks/Sprint-01/FEAT-003-project-structure-fsd.md`) from `coding-standards.md` §3, then checked item-by-item against FEAT-001's actual implementation rather than assumed complete. Result: 5 of 6 requirements already satisfied by FEAT-001 (folder skeleton, coarse import-direction rule, `import/no-cycle` equivalent, layer-ownership non-violation, folder-naming N/A-until-slices-exist); the sibling-feature-import isolation rule (§3.2) remains unimplemented and was left that way rather than built speculatively — recorded as technical debt (above) with its priority reassessed (rises to Medium-High once Sprint 2 adds a second feature). Formally recorded four ADRs (ADR-019 through ADR-022 in `docs/decisions.md`) covering the ESLint-over-oxlint choice, the eslint-plugin-import-x substitution, the verified eslint-plugin-boundaries configuration (including the specific pitfalls found: README/actual-schema mismatch, `mode: 'folder'` vs `mode: 'full'`, and the silent no-op without a resolver configured), and the sibling-feature-isolation deferral rationale. No new implementation work was started — this was a review-and-document-only pass.

- **2026-07-25 (`AUTH-004`):** Implemented Password Reset. `authRepository.requestPasswordReset`/`resetPassword` already existed from `AUTH-001`, so this ticket was Service validation + hooks + forms + pages + routing around them, following the `RegisterForm`/`LoginForm` pattern exactly. `ForgotPasswordForm` deliberately never branches on whether the submitted email is real — the same "check your email" message replaces the form on any successful mutation, matching the acceptance criteria's anti-enumeration requirement. `ResetPasswordPage` gates on `useAuth()`'s `profile`: a genuine emailed link auto-establishes a temporary session via supabase-js's own default `detectSessionInUrl` before the page renders, so `profile` being null once loading settles means an invalid/expired/already-used link — shown as a plain message with a link back to `/forgot-password`, not a crash or a silently-broken form. Found two small real gaps while exploring the existing code (fixed here, not deferred): `PATHS.public` had no `resetPassword` entry even though the repository's own `redirectTo` already pointed at `/reset-password`; and `mapSupabaseError.ts` never mapped Supabase's `otp_expired` code to the `RESET_TOKEN_EXPIRED` `ErrorCode` that already existed and already had a friendly message defined — meaning an expired reset link would previously have shown the wrong (generic invalid-credentials) message. Verified against the local Supabase stack: `ResetPasswordForm.test.tsx` signs a fresh user up directly (establishing the same "active session" precondition a real recovery-link click leaves the app in — the link→session handoff itself is supabase-js's own tested default, not re-verified here) then submits a new password and proves the change by confirming the *old* password now fails and the *new* one works; `ForgotPasswordForm.test.tsx` proves a real registered email and a nonexistent one produce the exact same confirmation text. One test needed an explicit longer `it(...)` timeout (Vitest's default 5000ms) since a fresh Docker/Postgres cold-start pushed the round trip past it — not a flake in the app code. 41 tests total, all passing; `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all pass clean. Docker Desktop had to be started this session before `supabase start` would work (it wasn't running).

- **2026-07-27 (production registration bug):** The developer reported production registration failing with `"Registration succeeded but no session was returned"` immediately after Sprint 2's frontend code went live. Traced to `auth.repository.ts:51-52`'s explicit guard, which fires when `supabase.auth.signUp()` returns a `user` with no `session` — the classic signature of email-confirmation being required before a session is issued. Checked `supabase/config.toml`: `enable_confirmations = false` is set for the local stack specifically because `user-stories.md`'s `AUTH-001` requires the user be "signed in" immediately on registration, with no verification gate. Confirmed via `git`/CLI (`supabase projects list` showed the CLI already linked and authenticated from a prior session) that `supabase db push`/`config push` are separate operations — only migrations had ever been pushed, never Auth config — so the remote project had silently been running on Supabase Cloud's own default (email confirmation on) since the day it was created. Chose not to run `supabase config push` to fix it, since `config.toml`'s `site_url`/`additional_redirect_urls` are still `127.0.0.1` local-dev values and pushing the whole file would have overwritten production's real redirect URLs (a much worse outcome than the bug being fixed). Recommended and the developer performed a scoped Dashboard toggle instead (Authentication → Providers → Email → "Confirm email" off). No Chrome browser connection was available to verify directly, so verified via a direct `curl` against the production Auth/PostgREST endpoints instead: `POST /auth/v1/signup` returned a real session immediately, and a follow-up `GET /rest/v1/profiles` using that session's own token confirmed `handle_new_user` had created the row (`role: "customer"`). The developer then confirmed the same result in an actual browser. Logged the underlying local/remote Auth-config parity gap as new **Technical Debt** (not just the one setting) since other `[auth]`/`[auth.email]` settings could have the same undetected drift. Updated Sprint 2 DoD, Recent Changes, Deployment Status, and Next Recommended Action accordingly.

Verified the fix three ways: the production build now shows each page as its own 0.2–5.7KB chunk with zero bundler warnings; the full test suite passes (86/86 — the same 3 pre-existing tests timed out under load and were re-confirmed transient in isolation, a now-familiar pattern this session); and a local `vite preview` + real-browser check confirmed `/` and `/properties` both still render correctly through the new `Suspense` boundary. Investigated why: Total Blocking Time stayed flat (~1.1–1.3s) across all three runs, and a quick check (grep for `import * as` full-library imports, `du -sh` on the largest installed packages) found no obvious single culprit — the ~262KB compressed payload is the ordinary combined weight of React/Supabase/TanStack Query/React Router/Radix/Zod, which every page needs regardless of which page-specific code gets split out. Page-level code splitting was the wrong lever for a bundle whose size is dominated by shared vendor code, not page-specific code; the extra network round-trip per lazy chunk may even be a net cost. - **2026-08-04 (per-role dashboard restructuring — three real redirect/layout bugs found and fixed across the session):** The developer reported the previous day's redirect fix wasn't actually working ("the logn page does not redirect after authentication... I have to manually redirect") and that dashboard pages rendered two navbars, then asked for a specific restructuring: remove the shared `/admin` shell, give every role its own dashboard endpoint. Investigated rather than assumed: found a genuine `useLogin` cache-race (fire-and-forget `invalidateQueries` losing a timing race against the caller's `navigate()` — invisible locally, reproducible against real network latency, explaining why the developer's remote-project testing hit it and Sprint 2's original local-only verification never did) and confirmed the double-navbar was structural (every dashboard nested inside `AppLayout`, which always renders the public `Header`). Presented a full plan — routes, per-role dashboard feature lists, the technical fix for both bugs — and used `AskUserQuestion` to resolve three genuine product decisions (legacy-URL handling: remove outright; customer dashboard: full sidebar shell; `/profile`: stays one shared route) before writing any code. Built: `paths.constants.ts` restructured into four dashboard groups; one shared `DashboardShell` widget reused by four thin per-role layouts (crossed the "extract on 3rd duplicate" threshold immediately with 2 new shells needed alongside the 2 existing ones); `routes.tsx` restructured so all four dashboard groups are siblings of `AppLayout`, not children — the actual double-navbar fix; `useLogin.ts`'s cache-race fixed with a synchronous `setQueryData`; `AgentDashboardLayout`/`AdminDashboardLayout` simplified (lost mechanisms — the `children`/`Outlet` duality, role-filtered nav links — that only existed because of the old shared-URL constraints); new `ModeratorDashboardOverviewPage`/`UserDashboardOverviewPage` pages; old `DashboardPage.tsx` retired. Verified via `claude-in-chrome` against a real local dev server — and during that verification pass, found a **third** real bug live in the browser: logging out of an agent account and into a moderator account landed the moderator on the guest homepage, not `/moderator-dashboard`. Diagnosed methodically (confirmed the account's DB role really was `'moderator'` before suspecting the code), traced it to `LoginForm` honoring `location.state.from` unconditionally regardless of whether the *new* role could reach that URL, and fixed it with `isReachableByRole()` plus a regression test reproducing the exact scenario. Also hit and worked around two `claude-in-chrome` tooling issues (screenshot/viewport-reporting, already logged since Sprint 4; a new ref-staleness issue where a `find`-returned ref silently no-ops after an intervening `form_input` call, worked around with literal-coordinate clicks) — neither blocked verification, both used `get_page_text`/`read_page` as the established fallback. Fixed one incidental, pre-existing test-timing fragility found while re-verifying (`AgentDashboardOverviewPage.test.tsx`'s untimed `findAllByText('0')`), confirmed via diffing the file that it predated this session. `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean; full suite 305/310, all 5 remaining failures matching the established pre-existing contention class, `LoginForm.test.tsx` itself re-confirmed passing 100% in isolation. `architecture.md` §6 rewritten, new `decisions.md` **ADR-031** added. **Did not commit, push, or deploy** — per established pattern, a separate, later, explicitly-requested step.

Investigated each claim before building anything: the dropdown bug traced to `--color-popover`/`--color-popover-foreground` never being defined (`Select`/`DropdownMenu` both use `bg-popover`, invalid and fully transparent in Tailwind v4 when undefined); `database.md` had already anticipated the 47-counties ask verbatim, just deferred; `database.md` also explicitly documents `locations` as a lookup table specifically to prevent free-text duplication, which shaped the Location design (a "create new" combobox that inserts a real row, not free text on the property itself). Used `AskUserQuestion` on the one real open call — geocoding provider for the map search — before writing any map code; developer picked Nominatim (free, no key) over a paid provider or skipping search. Built a shared `Combobox` (Popover + `cmdk`, two new small deps) and a `LocationPickerMap`/`LocationPickerMapCanvas` pair mirroring the existing read-only map's lazy-Leaflet split exactly. A mid-task message arrived ("while at it, build admin crud for users") — acknowledged, queued as its own follow-up task rather than context-switching mid-edit, with an upfront flag that Create/Delete of a real account needs Supabase's Admin API (`service_role`), likely a new Edge Function, not yet planned in detail. Verified: a new real-Supabase RLS integration test for the new `locations_insert_agent` policy (17/17 passing, including proving via `.select()`'s returned rows — not just `.error` — that the agent grant doesn't silently extend to update/delete); 4 new `PropertyForm.test.tsx` cases (8/8 passing); a new jsdom `scrollIntoView` polyfill `cmdk` needed. `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` clean, confirmed via build output that the new Leaflet/cmdk/Popover code stayed lazy-loaded (eager bundle size unchanged); full suite 314/316, the 2 failures matching the exact pre-existing flaky pair this project has documented repeatedly, neither file touched here.
