- **2026-08-04 (Admin User CRUD planned, then built — the project's first Edge Functions):** Picked up the queued "build admin crud for users" request from the entry above. Investigated first rather than assuming a shape: List and role/active-status Update already existed from Sprint 7; Create and Delete of a real `auth.users` account need Supabase's Admin API, which requires `service_role` — RLS/PostgREST can't reach `auth.users`, and this project has never put `service_role` anywhere near the frontend — so both genuinely need a new Edge Function, this project's first. Presented a plan and used `AskUserQuestion` on the two real open product decisions before writing any code: Create via email invite (`auth.admin.inviteUserByEmail`, chosen over asking the admin to set a password directly) and Delete as a hard delete only when the account is genuinely empty (chosen over a soft-deactivate-only approach or a cascading delete). Built `_shared/adminAuth.ts`'s `requireAdmin()` helper first (JWT validation → admin-role check → `service_role` client handoff), then `admin-invite-user` and `admin-delete-user` on top of it, verified each directly against the local stack via `supabase functions serve` + `curl` before touching any frontend code — this is what caught the first real bug: a seeded agent with 7 `properties.verified_by` rows (no properties/bookings/reviews of their own) returned a raw 500 instead of a clean 409, because `enforce_verification_authority()`'s trigger blocks even a cascaded `SET NULL` to `verified_by` outside its two sanctioned RPCs, a case the blocker check hadn't queried; fixed by adding a 5th check, re-verified via curl. Mid-build, the developer added a third requirement ("Admin should also be able to update user details") — recognized as materially simpler than Invite/Delete (plain `profiles` columns, no `auth.users` involvement, no new Edge Function needed) and folded into the same task via the existing `AdminUpdateUserSchema`/Repository/Service path rather than treated as scope creep. Built the frontend stack (Repository's `invokeAdminFunction()` Edge-Function-error parser, schemas, hooks, `AdminInviteUserDialog`/`DeleteUserDialog`/`EditUserDetailsDialog`, `AdminUserTable` wiring), wrote unit/component/Deno tests alongside each piece, then moved to final manual verification: real curl tests confirmed the 401/403 auth boundary and a real invite email captured in Mailpit; the browser pass confirmed Invite and Delete working end to end, but clicking "Edit" repeatedly showed a focus highlight with no dialog appearing and no error-boundary fallback either — narrowed by checking the console (zero errors) before suspecting the app itself, then found via `npm run typecheck` (clean) that the type system had no way to catch it, and only by writing a test that actually mounts `EditUserDetailsDialog` did the real cause surface: `AdminUpdateUserSchema.pick({...})` throws at runtime because the schema is built with `.refine()`, which Zod's `.pick()` explicitly can't operate on — the dialog was crashing silently inside a `RouteErrorBoundary` boundary that a same-pathname re-navigation likely never remounted, explaining why no visible fallback appeared either. Fixed with a new standalone `EditUserDetailsSchema`, re-confirmed via a screenshot showing the dialog open, pre-filled, and a real "User details updated." toast after submitting. Closed out with a full validation pass: `npm run lint` (0 errors), `npm run typecheck` (clean), `npx vitest run` (330/330, the only 2 failures in a full concurrent run being the pre-existing `PropertiesPage`/`LoginForm` contention flakes, both confirmed passing in isolation), `npm run build` (clean). Stopped the dev server and ran a clean `supabase db reset` to leave the local environment in a known-good state. `api-design.md`/`database.md`/`decisions.md` (new ADR-032) updated in the same change. **Did not commit, push, or deploy.**

Investigated the existing `VerificationActionDialog` first — confirmed it really did only ever show a title, nothing to check against. Presented two real design options via `AskUserQuestion` (a bigger inline Sheet/panel vs. a dedicated review page reusing the public listing-detail layout) before writing code; developer picked the dedicated page. Found the data layer already had everything needed — `propertyRepository.getByIdAdmin(id)` existed since Sprint 7 (only ever consumed internally by `verificationRepository.history()`) and RLS's `properties_select_all_moderator_admin` policy was already the real authority — so this was a pure UI-layer addition: one thin `adminVerificationService.getById()` passthrough, a `useVerificationProperty` hook, a new `AdminVerificationReviewPage` (routed at both the admin- and moderator-dashboard verification-queue `:id` paths, mirroring the queue page's own existing dual-mount precedent) reusing `PropertyGallery`/`PriceBreakdown`/`PropertyMap`/`AmenitiesGrid`/`AgentCard`/`VerificationBadge` in the exact order `PropertyDetailPage` already established, and a new `VerificationActionBar` (reason + Reject/Approve, sticky-on-mobile/in-flow-on-desktop like `ViewingCTA`) replacing the deleted `VerificationActionDialog`. **Two real, unrelated issues surfaced during this task's own validation, both fixed before calling it done:** (1) the new integration test's own fixtures had no cleanup — caught by a live manual browser pass that found the real Verification Queue cluttered with leftover "Review Test..." rows, fixed with `afterAll` cleanup mirroring `verification.rls.test.ts`'s existing pattern; (2) a full-suite test run immediately after `supabase db reset` showed mass, unrelated failures — traced to the `vector` container restart-looping (the exact same class of issue Sprint 8 already documented once for `auth`/`vector`), confirmed the DB/Auth/REST layer itself was healthy throughout and `vector` is just Supabase's log-shipping sidecar, resolved with a full `stop`+`start` cycle. - **2026-08-04 (session-management discussion, then four hardening pieces built and sequenced — ADR-033):** Asked to "talk about session management in this app" — walked through `AuthProvider`'s single-cache design, `ProtectedRoute`'s UX-only role, and this project's own two prior real bugs in the area (the `useLogin` cache-race, `LoginForm`'s `state.from` reachability bug). The developer then named a genuine pain point from their own manual testing — unable to have admin/agent/customer all signed in in the same browser at once — and asked for best-practice recommendations given Supabase Auth specifically, then to sequence and build them. Presented recommendations across three tiers (a dev-testing fix; concrete Supabase-specific hardening; lower-priority items), then sequenced foundational-first: an Auth-config audit, sign-out-everywhere + idle timeout, the dev multi-actor tool. The audit surfaced one real, previously-undiscovered gap: `config.toml`'s server-side password policy was still Supabase's weak default (6 chars, no complexity) despite `passwordSchema`/`api-design.md` §14 already documenting 8-char+letter+digit since Sprint 2 — fixed and confirmed live via a raw `curl` weak-password signup now genuinely rejected; also produced a checklist for settings only checkable/settable on the production Dashboard (no API access from this session). Built "sign out of all other devices" (`scope: 'others'`, deliberately not `'global'` — a refinement made explicit to the developer as it was built, not silently swapped — so triggering it doesn't also sign out the device used to click it), verified against a genuine second Supabase session whose refresh token is confirmed revoked afterward. Built a 15-minute admin/moderator-only idle timeout with a 60-second warning — both the duration and warn-vs-silent behavior resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before writing code, since neither is a call the docs could resolve. Hit and fixed a real React Compiler lint error while building the idle-timer hook (`setState` called synchronously inside a mount `useEffect`) — restructured so only genuine callback-triggered resets touch state. For the dev multi-actor pain point, reconsidered the originally-planned bespoke testing tool mid-task in favor of a smaller, more elegant fix: swapping the Supabase client's session storage from `localStorage` to `sessionStorage` in `npm run dev` only (`env.isDev`, new) — since `sessionStorage` is scoped per-tab, this gives every dev-server tab an independent real session automatically, no new tooling. **Manually verified, not just unit-tested, at every step:** the weak-password rejection via curl; two real browser tabs simultaneously and independently signed in as `agent1.seed@rentalhunt.test`/`customer1.seed@rentalhunt.test` on the same origin — the exact scenario that started this whole task; a third tab (admin) hit repeated `claude-in-chrome` click-registration flakiness confirmed via network-request tracking (no auth call was ever sent) to be a tool issue, not an app bug, and was accepted as sufficient given the mechanism was already proven with two tabs. Verified overall: `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`build` all clean; `npx vitest run` — a genuinely clean 345/345, not even the usual pre-existing flake failing this time. `api-design.md` (new §5.11) and `ui-guidelines.md` (new §11.21) updated in the same change; `decisions.md` gained **ADR-033**. **Did not commit, push, or deploy** — per established pattern.

# Claude Instructions

**Before every coding session:**
1. Read this file in full.
2. Read the current sprint's section in `docs/roadmap.md`.
3. Continue from the **Current Task** / **Next Recommended Action** above — do not start unrelated work.
4. Update this file before finishing the session (see below).

**Update rules:**
- **Never overwrite history.** Completed Tasks, Recent Changes, and Session Notes are append-only logs — a past entry is never edited or deleted to reflect new information; a correction is a *new* entry noting the correction.
- **Append updates instead of replacing sections wholesale.** Move a task from "In Progress" to "Completed Tasks" rather than deleting it; add a new bullet to "Recent Changes" rather than rewriting the existing summary.
- **Maintain chronological order.** Every dated entry (Completed Tasks, Session Notes, Technical Debt) is added in date order, oldest first, at the end of its list.
- **Always update, in this order, at the end of a session:** Current Task → Completed Tasks (if finished) → In Progress / Blocked / Pending Decisions (if changed) → Recent Changes → Metrics → Session Notes → Next Recommended Action.
- **Keep dates in absolute `YYYY-MM-DD` form**, never relative ("today", "yesterday") — this file is read cold by sessions with no memory of when "today" was.
